### TensorAR: Refinement is All You Need in Autoregressive Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16324v1)
- **Authors**: Cheng Cheng, Lin Song, Yicheng Xiao, Yuxin Chen, Xuchong Zhang, Hongbin Sun, Ying Shan
- **Abstract**: Autoregressive (AR) image generators offer a language-model-friendly approach to image generation by predicting discrete image tokens in a causal sequence. However, unlike diffusion models, AR models lack a mechanism to refine previous predictions, limiting their generation quality. In this paper, we introduce TensorAR, a new AR paradigm that reformulates image generation from next-token prediction to next-tensor prediction. By generating overlapping windows of image patches (tensors) in a sliding fashion, TensorAR enables iterative refinement of previously generated content. To prevent information leakage during training, we propose a discrete tensor noising scheme, which perturbs input tokens via codebook-indexed noise. TensorAR is implemented as a plug-and-play module compatible with existing AR models. Extensive experiments on LlamaGEN, Open-MAGVIT2, and RAR demonstrate that TensorAR significantly improves the generation performance of autoregressive models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TensorAR, a new autoregressive (AR) image generation paradigm. It moves away from the standard "next-token prediction" approach to a "next-tensor prediction" approach. Instead of predicting single image patches sequentially, TensorAR predicts overlapping windows of image patches (tensors). This overlapping design allows later predictions to refine previously generated patches, mimicking the iterative refinement process seen in diffusion models. To prevent information leakage during training (since some tokens in the predicted tensor are already present in the input), the authors propose a discrete tensor noising scheme that adds codebook-indexed noise to the input tensors. They implement TensorAR as a plug-and-play module and demonstrate its effectiveness by integrating it into existing AR models like LlamaGEN, Open-MAGVIT2, and RAR, showing improved image generation performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of refining previously generated tokens in an autoregressive image generation framework is relatively novel, especially the implementation using a "sliding window" of overlapping tensors. While diffusion models achieve refinement inherently, explicitly introducing this into AR models is a valuable contribution. The discrete noising scheme tailored for this architecture also shows some novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The significance lies in bridging the gap between AR models and diffusion models in terms of image quality. AR models are attractive due to their compatibility with language models, but have lagged behind in generation fidelity. TensorAR offers a way to boost the performance of existing AR architectures without extensive retraining or architectural redesign. The reported improvements in FID score, while modest, suggest a real step forward.  The plug-and-play nature of the TensorAR module is also a significant advantage, making it easy to integrate into existing workflows.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly identifies a limitation of AR models (lack of refinement) and proposes a targeted solution.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:**  TensorAR and the discrete noising scheme are clearly explained.
    *   **Effective Implementation:** The plug-and-play module design is practical.
    *   **Solid Experimental Results:**  The paper shows consistent improvements across different AR models and ablation studies providing insights.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the improvements are consistent, they aren't revolutionary.  The improvements in FID score might not always translate to a noticeable improvement in visual quality. Also, other metrics such as IS, Precision and Recall sometimes show conflicting results.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The experiments are primarily focused on class-conditional ImageNet generation. While this is a standard benchmark, the results might not generalize to other datasets or generation tasks (e.g., text-to-image generation, higher resolution images, or more complex scenes). Also, it is not clear if TensorAR would add value to current state-of-the-art image generation models.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper mentions increased latency. A more detailed analysis of the computational overhead compared to standard AR models would be beneficial.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:** The paper lacks a theoretical justification for using a discrete noise type.

*   **Potential Impact:**  The paper could potentially influence future research by inspiring more work on incorporating refinement mechanisms into AR models. Its plug-and-play design makes it potentially useful for practitioners working with existing AR architectures. It may also push the development of new, more efficient and powerful hybrid models combining the strengths of AR and diffusion paradigms.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the above analysis, a score of **7** seems appropriate. TensorAR presents a novel and well-executed approach to address a specific limitation of autoregressive image generation. The consistent experimental results and modular design make it a potentially valuable contribution to the field. While the improvements are not drastic, they're meaningful and the method provides a direction for improving AR image generation techniques. Also, the improvements in FID, Precision, Recall and IS metrics are not consistent. The limited scope of the experiments and lack of deeper theoretical analysis prevents it from being scored higher.

**Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ChemMLLM: Chemical Multimodal Large Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16326v1)
- **Authors**: Qian Tan, Dongzhan Zhou, Peng Xia, Wanhao Liu, Wanli Ouyang, Lei Bai, Yuqiang Li, Tianfan Fu
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have made impressive progress in many applications in recent years. However, chemical MLLMs that can handle cross-modal understanding and generation remain underexplored. To fill this gap, in this paper, we propose ChemMLLM, a unified chemical multimodal large language model for molecule understanding and generation. Also, we design five multimodal tasks across text, molecular SMILES strings, and image, and curate the datasets. We benchmark ChemMLLM against a range of general leading MLLMs and Chemical LLMs on these tasks. Experimental results show that ChemMLLM achieves superior performance across all evaluated tasks. For example, in molecule image optimization task, ChemMLLM outperforms the best baseline (GPT-4o) by 118.9\% (4.27 vs 1.95 property improvement). The code is publicly available at https://github.com/bbsbz/ChemMLLM.git.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ChemMLLM paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ChemMLLM, a chemical multimodal large language model designed for understanding and generating molecules across text, SMILES strings, and image modalities. To this end, the authors curate five multimodal chemistry tasks and datasets encompassing generation and comprehension. ChemMLLM is built upon a novel "Image Tokenizer-LLM-Image De-tokenizer" architecture, utilizing a finetuned Vector Quantized Generative Adversarial Network (VQGAN) for molecule image representation. They propose a two-stage training strategy: Mol-VQGAN pre-training followed by ChemMLLM supervised fine-tuning. Experimental results demonstrate ChemMLLM's superior performance compared to general and chemical MLLMs across the evaluated tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in unifying multimodal molecule understanding and generation within a single MLLM framework. While individual components (VQGAN, Transformer-based LLM) are not novel, their integration for this specific application and the introduction of a comprehensive suite of tasks and datasets constitutes a significant contribution. Previous chemical MLLMs have primarily focused on understanding tasks or have not directly generated molecular images.
    The "Image Tokenizer-LLM-Image De-tokenizer" architecture, while drawing inspiration from previous work (e.g., Chameleon), is adapted for the specific characteristics of molecular images, addressing challenges related to sparsity and linearity. The curated multimodal datasets are also a valuable resource for the community.

*   **Significance:** ChemMLLM has the potential to impact various areas within cheminformatics, including drug discovery and materials design, by facilitating more intuitive and visual interactions with AI models. The ability to generate molecular images directly from text or SMILES strings allows chemists to communicate and reason more naturally. The unified framework can potentially streamline workflows and enable new research avenues.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive task suite and dataset curation.
    *   Well-defined architecture and training strategy.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating superior performance.
    *   Clear articulation of the challenges and solutions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Relatively small scale of the LLM. While the performance is impressive, further scaling might lead to even better results. It builds upon a 7B parameter LLM, which is smaller than the largest language models available today.
    *   Limited exploration of real-world applications. While the authors suggest potential applications, more concrete examples or case studies would strengthen the paper.
    *   The study could benefit from more in-depth ablation experiments on the choice of particular architectural components and training strategies. More ablations on how to adapt a general vision-language model for this task should be useful.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's clear articulation of the challenges and the solutions provided, along with the open-source code and datasets, are likely to inspire further research in this area. ChemMLLM could serve as a foundation for developing more advanced chemical MLLMs with improved capabilities and broader applications.

* **Reason for Score:** While the paper builds upon existing techniques, its integration of multiple modalities and the introduction of novel tasks and datasets represent a significant step forward in chemical MLLMs. ChemMLLM addresses a notable gap in the field. The contribution has the potential to advance the field by enabling new research avenues. A score of 7 reflects the clear value in this work.

### SC4ANM: Identifying Optimal Section Combinations for Automated Novelty Prediction in Academic Papers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16330v1)
- **Authors**: Wenqing Wu, Chengzhi Zhang, Tong Bao, Yi Zhao
- **Abstract**: Novelty is a core component of academic papers, and there are multiple perspectives on the assessment of novelty. Existing methods often focus on word or entity combinations, which provide limited insights. The content related to a paper's novelty is typically distributed across different core sections, e.g., Introduction, Methodology and Results. Therefore, exploring the optimal combination of sections for evaluating the novelty of a paper is important for advancing automated novelty assessment. In this paper, we utilize different combinations of sections from academic papers as inputs to drive language models to predict novelty scores. We then analyze the results to determine the optimal section combinations for novelty score prediction. We first employ natural language processing techniques to identify the sectional structure of academic papers, categorizing them into introduction, methods, results, and discussion (IMRaD). Subsequently, we used different combinations of these sections (e.g., introduction and methods) as inputs for pretrained language models (PLMs) and large language models (LLMs), employing novelty scores provided by human expert reviewers as ground truth labels to obtain prediction results. The results indicate that using introduction, results and discussion is most appropriate for assessing the novelty of a paper, while the use of the entire text does not yield significant results. Furthermore, based on the results of the PLMs and LLMs, the introduction and results appear to be the most important section for the task of novelty score prediction. The code and dataset for this paper can be accessed at https://github.com/njust-winchy/SC4ANM.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SC4ANM: Identifying Optimal Section Combinations for Automated Novelty Prediction in Academic Papers" investigates the impact of different combinations of paper sections (Introduction, Methods, Results, Discussion - IMRaD) on the performance of automated novelty prediction models.  It explores the use of both fine-tuned pre-trained language models (PLMs) and large language models (LLMs) with varying section combinations as input to predict novelty scores provided by expert reviewers (acting as ground truth). The authors analyze which section combinations yield the most accurate predictions, aiming to optimize automated novelty assessment and identify the key sections that contribute most to a paper's perceived novelty. They find that using a combination of Introduction, Results, and Discussion (IRD) generally produces the best results. They also compare their approach against a traditional novelty measure based on citation analysis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a relevant problem: improving automated novelty assessment. The idea of evaluating different section combinations is a reasonable approach to enhance the models' focus and reduce noise. The comparison to a citation-based baseline is also valuable. However, the paper's novelty is somewhat limited. While exploring section combinations isn't entirely new, applying it specifically in the context of LLMs and recent PLMs for novelty *prediction* is a contribution. The application of LLMs and PLMs with various section combinations offers some level of novelty.

*   **Significance:** The work has the potential to contribute to several areas:

    *   **Automated Paper Review:** By identifying important sections, the study could help in creating more efficient and accurate automated paper review systems.
    *   **Research Evaluation:**  The findings could inform how researchers and institutions assess novelty during grant applications or performance evaluations.
    *   **NLP for Scientific Text:** The work contributes to a growing body of research on applying NLP techniques to understand and process scientific text.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Research Questions:** The paper clearly articulates its research questions and provides a logical flow.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The use of a real-world dataset of ICLR papers and reviewer scores strengthens the empirical validity.
    *   **Multiple Models:**  The exploration of both PLMs and LLMs provides a broader perspective.
    *   **Comparison to Baseline:** Including a traditional novelty assessment method provides a reference point.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The study is limited to ICLR papers, which may not be representative of all academic fields.  Generalizability is a concern. The claim that the data of ICLR aligns with the IMRD structure may be correct, but the authors did not prove it in this research.
    *   **LLM Implementation:** The use of LLMs is relatively basic (zero-shot prompting).  Fine-tuning or more sophisticated prompting strategies could potentially yield better results. It is well known that GPT-3.5 tends to provide somewhat biased responses; the lack of a way to avoid this is a weakness.
    *   **Effectiveness Metrics:** The evaluation metrics are relatively basic. Given that the paper addresses a problem related to evaluating novelty, a more comprehensive set of metrics (perhaps including metrics that capture the diversity of outputs or qualitative analysis) would strengthen the work. The inter annotator consistency (Table 4, Figure 10 of the reference paper) seems low.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could inspire further research in this area, particularly in developing more sophisticated NLP models for novelty assessment and exploring its applicability to diverse scientific domains. However, the practical impact depends on whether the findings can be generalized beyond the ICLR context.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a worthwhile but incremental contribution to the field of automated novelty assessment. The systematic exploration of section combinations is a reasonable approach, and the use of real-world data is a strength. However, the limited scope, basic LLM implementation, and the relative simplicity of the evaluation metrics somewhat restrict the impact. While the paper offers valuable insights, significant advancements are needed to develop practical, reliable automated novelty assessment systems. The contribution to the field appears incremental, rather than a breakthrough.

### Panoptic Captioning: Seeking An Equivalency Bridge for Image and Text
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16334v1)
- **Authors**: Kun-Yu Lin, Hongjun Wang, Weining Ren, Kai Han
- **Abstract**: This work introduces panoptic captioning, a novel task striving to seek the minimum text equivalence of images. We take the first step towards panoptic captioning by formulating it as a task of generating a comprehensive textual description for an image, which encapsulates all entities, their respective locations and attributes, relationships among entities, as well as global image state.Through an extensive evaluation, our work reveals that state-of-the-art Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have limited performance in solving panoptic captioning. To address this, we propose an effective data engine named PancapEngine to produce high-quality data and a novel method named PancapChain to improve panoptic captioning. Specifically, our PancapEngine first detects diverse categories of entities in images by an elaborate detection suite, and then generates required panoptic captions using entity-aware prompts. Additionally, our PancapChain explicitly decouples the challenging panoptic captioning task into multiple stages and generates panoptic captions step by step. More importantly, we contribute a comprehensive metric named PancapScore and a human-curated test set for reliable model evaluation.Experiments show that our PancapChain-13B model can beat state-of-the-art open-source MLLMs like InternVL-2.5-78B and even surpass proprietary models like GPT-4o and Gemini-2.0-Pro, demonstrating the effectiveness of our data engine and method. Project page: https://visual-ai.github.io/pancap/
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "panoptic captioning," a novel computer vision task aiming to find the minimum text equivalence of an image by generating comprehensive descriptions encompassing all entities, their locations, attributes, relationships, and global image state. It identifies limitations in existing Multi-Modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) for this task and proposes PancapEngine, a data engine, and PancapChain, a staged captioning method to address these limitations. The work also includes a new metric, PancapScore, and a human-curated test set for reliable evaluation. Experiments show that PancapChain outperforms state-of-the-art open-source and proprietary MLLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel task, panoptic captioning, with a clear motivation rooted in existing image captioning limitations. Formulating image representation as seeking minimal text equivalence is a significant step. Developing PancapScore and a testing pipeline are valuable contributions. The novelty stems from the integrated approach of the new task, data engine, staged methodology, and dedicated evaluation metric.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in several aspects:
    *   **Addressing a Gap:** It directly addresses the lack of comprehensive image descriptions in existing captioning tasks.
    *   **Benchmarking & Evaluation:** The SA-Pancap benchmark and PancapScore are highly useful for future research.
    *   **Improved Performance:** The PancapChain method's outperformance demonstrates the potential of staged approaches to complex vision-language tasks. The improved performance over multiple models (including GPT-4o) is significant.
    *   **Text Representation:** It provides a methodology for creating text representation for Images that can be used for downstream tasks

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem formulation and well-defined task scope.
    *   Comprehensive approach, including data generation, method design, and evaluation.
    *   The design and execution of experiments demonstrating the limitations of existing MLLMs and the superiority of the proposed approach.
    *   The thorough ablation study demonstrates the effectiveness of individual modules within PancapChain.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the "minimum text equivalence" concept is interesting, the paper's formulation of panoptic captioning still involves a relatively lengthy description. The authors acknowledge it's an approximation, but further exploration of truly minimal representations would be valuable.
    *   The reliance on LLMs for semantic content extraction introduces a potential dependency and raises questions about error propagation. The human analysis partly mitigates this but further validation of the extraction process could be considered.
    *   The models outperform each other largely in one specific dataset (SA-Pancap), with limited generalization.
    *   Broader impact section could more deeply explore ethical aspects beyond bias in the data.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper will likely stimulate research in comprehensive image understanding, vision-language alignment, and structured image representations. The SA-Pancap benchmark and PancapScore will likely become important resources for the community.
    The demonstration on image-text retrieval proves that it can be used for other downstream applications

**Justification:**

The paper introduces a genuinely new task with clear benefits and provides a complete and well-executed solution. The contributions of the data engine, method, and metric are substantial. While there are minor limitations regarding the true minimality of the text representation and potential biases, the overall contribution is significant and opens up new avenues for research in the field. The work provides a significant advance over existing approaches and demonstrates the potential of more structured and comprehensive image representations.

### FPQVAR: Floating Point Quantization for Visual Autoregressive Model with FPGA Hardware Co-design
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16335v1)
- **Authors**: Renjie Wei, Songqiang Xu, Qingyu Guo, Meng Li
- **Abstract**: Visual autoregressive (VAR) modeling has marked a paradigm shift in image generation from next-token prediction to next-scale prediction. VAR predicts a set of tokens at each step from coarse to fine scale, leading to better image quality and faster inference speed compared to existing diffusion models. However, the large parameter size and computation cost hinder its deployment on edge devices. To reduce the memory and computation cost, we propose FPQVAR, an efficient post-training floating-point (FP) quantization framework for VAR featuring algorithm and hardware co-design. At the algorithm level, we first identify the challenges of quantizing VAR. To address them, we propose Dual Format Quantization for the highly imbalanced input activation. We further propose Group-wise Hadamard Transformation and GHT-Aware Learnable Transformation to address the time-varying outlier channels. At the hardware level, we design the first low-bit FP quantizer and multiplier with lookup tables on FPGA and propose the first FPGA-based VAR accelerator featuring low-bit FP computation and an elaborate two-level pipeline. Extensive experiments show that compared to the state-of-the-art quantization method, our proposed FPQVAR significantly improves Fr\'echet Inception Distance (FID) from 10.83 to 3.58, Inception Score (IS) from 175.9 to 241.5 under 4-bit quantization. FPQVAR also significantly improves the performance of 6-bit quantized VAR, bringing it on par with the FP16 model. Our accelerator on AMD-Xilinx VCK190 FPGA achieves a throughput of 1.1 image/s, which is 3.1x higher than the integer-based accelerator. It also demonstrates 3.6x and 2.8x higher energy efficiency compared to the integer-based accelerator and GPU baseline, respectively.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FPQVAR: Floating Point Quantization for Visual Autoregressive Model with FPGA Hardware Co-design":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes FPQVAR, a post-training floating-point (FP) quantization framework designed to reduce the memory and computation costs of Visual Autoregressive (VAR) models, which are used for image generation.  The framework incorporates both algorithmic and hardware co-design. At the algorithm level, it introduces: 1) Dual Format Quantization (DFQ) to handle the imbalanced activation distribution of the FC2 layer; 2) Group-wise Hadamard Transformation (GHT) and GHT-Aware Learnable Transformation (GALT) to address time-varying outlier channels.  On the hardware side, the paper designs an efficient low-bit FP quantizer and multiplier implemented on an FPGA, along with a VAR accelerator featuring low-bit FP computation and a two-level pipeline. Experiments demonstrate improved Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) and Inception Score (IS) compared to existing methods under low-bit quantization and a speedup on FPGA compared to integer implementations and GPU baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**
    *   **Algorithm:** The introduction of DFQ, GHT, and GALT demonstrates algorithmic novelty. Addressing the specific challenges of VAR quantization (imbalanced activations, outlier channels with temporal variation) is commendable. While techniques like Hadamard transformations and learnable scaling factors exist in other contexts (LLMs, etc.), their application and adaptation to VAR, especially with the "group-wise" approach and GHT-Aware learning, constitute a novel contribution. DFQ specifically targeted to imbalanced activations of FC2 is also notable.
    *   **Hardware:** Designing a low-bit FP quantizer and multiplier *specifically* for FPGA implementation *for VAR* is a significant hardware contribution.  The two-level pipeline architecture and hardware-algorithm co-design further enhance novelty.  The lack of prior work on low-bit FP inference for VAR on FPGA justifies this aspect's novelty.
    *   **Overall:** The combination of algorithm *and* hardware innovations targeted *specifically* at the challenging VAR architecture increases the overall novelty.
*   **Significance:**
    *   **Improved Image Generation:**  The significant improvements in FID and IS demonstrate a positive impact on image generation quality under low-bit quantization, making VAR models more practical for resource-constrained devices.
    *   **FPGA Acceleration:**  The speedup and energy efficiency achieved on FPGA over integer implementations and GPU baselines are practically significant. This shows a pathway towards deploying VAR models on edge devices with limited power and computational resources.
    *   **FP for resource-constrained platforms:** It demonstrated that FP quantization can provide a good trade-off than INT, which generally requires retraining and the result model's quality is always worse. This paper shows that FP with proper framework can achieve similar quality with less computational overhead.
    *   **Wider Applicability**: Some of their algorithms may be able to transfer to another visual models, like diffusion model.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:**  The paper tackles the VAR quantization problem holistically, considering both algorithmic and hardware aspects.
    *   **Problem-Specific Solutions:** The proposed techniques are tailored to the specific characteristics and challenges of VAR models.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of FPQVAR in terms of image quality, speed, and energy efficiency.
    *   **Clear Explanations:** The paper explains the proposed techniques and their rationale clearly.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The optimizations are highly tailored to VAR. It is not immediately clear how easily the DFQ, GHT, and GALT techniques could be transferred to *other* image generation models or other deep learning architectures. However, the idea of quantizing different parts of the network using different techniques/algorithm can be utilized, thus the "spirit" of these techniques can still be generalizable.
    *   **FPGA Specificity:**  The hardware design is targeted explicitly for FPGA. The transferability of the hardware design to other platforms (e.g., ASICs) is not explored.
    *   **Evaluation Scope:** The experiments focus on ImageNet conditional image generation. Wider evaluations on different datasets or tasks could strengthen the results.
    *   **Theoretical analysis:** The paper might benefit from a deeper theoretical analysis of the quantization errors, especially those caused by GHT, and a more formal justification for the choices of FP formats.
    *   **Comparison scope**: Compare with other quantization methods instead of only RTN and LiteVAR, such as methods in paper [31].

*   **Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

*   **Spurring Further Research:** It opens avenues for further research into efficient quantization techniques for VAR models and hardware acceleration.
*   **Practical Deployments:** It enables the deployment of VAR models on edge devices, expanding their application scope.
*   **Algorithm-Hardware Co-design:** The co-design approach provides inspiration for other research efforts aiming to optimize deep learning models for specific hardware platforms.
*   **Pushing beyond INt quantization**: This paper demonstrated a good balance between algorithm and hardware by quantizing using FP on FPGA. This inspires other researchers to push beyond INT.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty and significance of this work, the paper deserves a strong score. The targeted approach, combining algorithmic optimizations and hardware co-design for VAR models, provides a compelling path to enable efficient deployment of these models on resource-constrained platforms. The significant experimental results showing improved image quality, speedup, and energy efficiency justify a positive assessment. However, the limitations in generalizability and evaluation scope prevent it from achieving a top score.

### Improving Chemical Understanding of LLMs via SMILES Parsing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16340v1)
- **Authors**: Yunhui Jang, Jaehyung Kim, Sungsoo Ahn
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly recognized as powerful tools for scientific discovery, particularly in molecular science. A fundamental requirement for these models is the ability to accurately understand molecular structures, commonly encoded in the SMILES representation. However, current LLMs struggle to interpret SMILES, even failing to carry out basic tasks such as counting molecular rings. To address this limitation, we introduce CLEANMOL, a novel framework that formulates SMILES parsing into a suite of clean and deterministic tasks explicitly designed to promote graph-level molecular comprehension. These tasks span from subgraph matching to global graph matching, providing structured supervision aligned with molecular structural properties. We construct a molecular pretraining dataset with adaptive difficulty scoring and pre-train open-source LLMs on these tasks. Our results show that CLEANMOL not only enhances structural comprehension but also achieves the best or competes with the baseline on the Mol-Instructions benchmark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitations of Large Language Models (LLMs) in understanding and interpreting SMILES strings, a common representation of molecular structures. The authors argue that current LLMs struggle with SMILES parsing due to the complex syntactic rules and structural constraints inherent in the representation. To overcome this, they introduce CLEANMOL, a framework that formulates SMILES parsing as a suite of clean, deterministic, and scalable tasks. These tasks, ranging from subgraph matching to global graph matching, provide structured supervision to promote graph-level molecular comprehension. The authors construct a pretraining dataset (CLEANMOL dataset) and pre-train open-source LLMs on these tasks. The results demonstrate that CLEANMOL enhances structural comprehension and achieves competitive or state-of-the-art results on the Mol-Instructions benchmark.  The paper also includes a task-adaptive data pruning and curriculum learning strategy to improve training efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of framing SMILES parsing as a set of explicit, deterministic tasks is a solid and potentially valuable contribution. While other works have addressed LLMs for chemical tasks, this paper’s emphasis on structural comprehension as a foundational skill and its direct translation into a pretraining objective is relatively novel.  The data pruning and curriculum learning approach, while not entirely new concepts, are thoughtfully applied within the chemical domain to improve data efficiency. However, The novelty could be limited depending on how other papers (such as Jang et al., 2024 cited by the authors) have specifically approached the task. The devil is in the details, and without fully understanding the fine details of the other related works, this makes it difficult to comment definitively on the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The work has significant implications for applying LLMs in chemistry. By improving SMILES parsing, the framework can lead to more reliable and accurate molecular reasoning. The empirical results on the Mol-Instructions benchmark are strong evidence of the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The demonstration that the framework improves performance even without direct exposure to downstream task data highlights its ability to enhance general molecular understanding. The open-sourcing of the CLEANMOL dataset and models will enable further research and development in this area. However, there are limits to the significance. The results, while promising, are still within the context of existing benchmarks. There is a question of whether the pre-training tasks sufficiently capture the complexities of molecular understanding. Performance on highly curated benchmarks may not always translate into real-world applications, and there is a need to rigorously test the impact of CLEANMOL across diverse cheminformatics tasks, like de novo drug discovery, etc. This is critical, because a potential real-world task might require information regarding 3D conformation or other nuance features that current setup of the model is not able to capture.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-defined and clear problem statement.
    *   A novel and theoretically sound approach to SMILES parsing.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple benchmarks.
    *   Demonstrated performance improvement over baselines.
    *   Open-sourcing of data and models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited scope of structural information (no 3D information or more nuanced chemical features).
    *   Potential language-specific limitations (experiments only in English).
    *   Experiment limited to model scales up to ~8B parameters. It's hard to determine if this scales to very large models.
    *   The novelty is relative, since there are other papers exploring graph level molecular comprehension.

* **Impact/Potential Influence:** The paper's potential influence is good, but not revolutionary. This work is likely to be well-cited and used as a basis for further research. However, it's not likely to radically change the field overnight.

**Rationale for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, and the relative novelty compared to existing work and the impact within the field, a score of 7 is assigned. The paper presents a good and well-executed approach to an important problem, but its limitations and the need for broader validation prevent it from achieving a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Embodied Agents Meet Personalization: Exploring Memory Utilization for Personalized Assistance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16348v1)
- **Authors**: Taeyoon Kwon, Dongwook Choi, Sunghwan Kim, Hyojun Kim, Seungjun Moon, Beong-woo Kwak, Kuan-Hao Huang, Jinyoung Yeo
- **Abstract**: Embodied agents empowered by large language models (LLMs) have shown strong performance in household object rearrangement tasks. However, these tasks primarily focus on single-turn interactions with simplified instructions, which do not truly reflect the challenges of providing meaningful assistance to users. To provide personalized assistance, embodied agents must understand the unique semantics that users assign to the physical world (e.g., favorite cup, breakfast routine) by leveraging prior interaction history to interpret dynamic, real-world instructions. Yet, the effectiveness of embodied agents in utilizing memory for personalized assistance remains largely underexplored. To address this gap, we present MEMENTO, a personalized embodied agent evaluation framework designed to comprehensively assess memory utilization capabilities to provide personalized assistance. Our framework consists of a two-stage memory evaluation process design that enables quantifying the impact of memory utilization on task performance. This process enables the evaluation of agents' understanding of personalized knowledge in object rearrangement tasks by focusing on its role in goal interpretation: (1) the ability to identify target objects based on personal meaning (object semantics), and (2) the ability to infer object-location configurations from consistent user patterns, such as routines (user patterns). Our experiments across various LLMs reveal significant limitations in memory utilization, with even frontier models like GPT-4o experiencing a 30.5% performance drop when required to reference multiple memories, particularly in tasks involving user patterns. These findings, along with our detailed analyses and case studies, provide valuable insights for future research in developing more effective personalized embodied agents. Project website: https://connoriginal.github.io/MEMENTO
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Embodied Agents Meet Personalization: Exploring Memory Utilization for Personalized Assistance" introduces MEMENTO, a novel framework for evaluating how well embodied agents leverage memory (specifically episodic memory) for personalized assistance in object rearrangement tasks. MEMENTO decomposes the evaluation into two stages: memory acquisition and memory utilization. This allows for isolating and quantifying the impact of memory usage on task performance. The framework also categorizes personalized knowledge into object semantics and user patterns, enabling a finer-grained analysis. Experiments conducted using various LLMs (including GPT-4o, Claude-3.5-Sonnet, Llama 3.1, and Qwen 2.5) reveal limitations in memory utilization, especially when dealing with multiple memories and user patterns. The paper identifies common failure modes and offers insights for future research in personalized embodied agents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty of Problem Formulation:** The paper addresses a crucial yet often overlooked aspect of embodied agents: providing personalized assistance beyond simple instruction-following. The focus on leveraging prior interactions to understand user-specific semantics and routines is timely and relevant.
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation Framework:** The MEMENTO framework provides a systematic way to assess memory utilization in embodied agents. The two-stage evaluation process and the categorization of personalized knowledge are valuable contributions for future research.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments are well-designed and executed, covering a range of LLMs with varying capabilities. The performance comparison between different models highlights the current limitations of memory utilization in embodied agents.
    *   **In-depth Analysis:** The paper goes beyond simply reporting results and offers detailed analyses of error cases, the impact of memory retrieval, and the role of personalized knowledge types. This provides valuable insights for future research directions.
    *   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the problem, framework, experiments, and results. The figures and tables are informative and support the discussion effectively.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Personalization:** While the categorization of personalized knowledge is a valuable contribution, it is somewhat limited in scope. Real-world personalization can involve more complex factors, such as user preferences beyond object semantics and routines, emotional context, and dynamic adaptation to changing user needs.
    *   **Simulated Environment:** The experiments are conducted in a simulated environment (Habitat 3.0). While this allows for controlled experiments, it may not fully capture the complexities of real-world human-robot interaction. Generalizability to real-world scenarios is not fully demonstrated.
    *   **Oracle Skills:** The reliance on oracle skills for low-level perception and motor control limits the scope of the evaluation. In real-world scenarios, these low-level capabilities can significantly impact the overall performance of embodied agents.
    *   **Evaluation Tasks:** The complexity of the object arrangement tasks, despite the use of PartNR and custom data, is inherently limited within the confines of the Habitat simulated environment. More intricate tasks, for example, requesting that the agent set up the coffee machine for brewing after fetching specific ingredients such as a specific mug, type of sugar, and blend, or a routine involving multiple complex steps across multiple rooms, would increase the significance of the study.

*   **Significance and Impact:**

    *   The paper highlights the importance of memory utilization for personalized assistance in embodied agents, an area that has been relatively underexplored.
    *   The MEMENTO framework provides a valuable tool for researchers to evaluate and compare the memory utilization capabilities of different embodied agents.
    *   The experimental results and analyses offer insights into the current limitations of LLM-powered embodied agents in leveraging memory, guiding future research directions.
    *   The paper opens up avenues for future work in developing more effective personalized embodied agents that can adapt to user-specific needs and preferences.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of embodied agents by addressing the critical aspect of memory utilization for personalized assistance. The MEMENTO framework provides a systematic way to evaluate and analyze memory capabilities, and the experimental results offer insights into the limitations of current LLM-powered embodied agents. While the paper has some limitations in scope and generalizability, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses. It provides a strong foundation for future research in developing more effective personalized embodied agents.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Style Transfer with Diffusion Models for Synthetic-to-Real Domain Adaptation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16360v1)
- **Authors**: Estelle Chigot, Dennis G. Wilson, Meriem Ghrib, Thomas Oberlin
- **Abstract**: Semantic segmentation models trained on synthetic data often perform poorly on real-world images due to domain gaps, particularly in adverse conditions where labeled data is scarce. Yet, recent foundation models enable to generate realistic images without any training. This paper proposes to leverage such diffusion models to improve the performance of vision models when learned on synthetic data. We introduce two novel techniques for semantically consistent style transfer using diffusion models: Class-wise Adaptive Instance Normalization and Cross-Attention (CACTI) and its extension with selective attention Filtering (CACTIF). CACTI applies statistical normalization selectively based on semantic classes, while CACTIF further filters cross-attention maps based on feature similarity, preventing artifacts in regions with weak cross-attention correspondences. Our methods transfer style characteristics while preserving semantic boundaries and structural coherence, unlike approaches that apply global transformations or generate content without constraints. Experiments using GTA5 as source and Cityscapes/ACDC as target domains show that our approach produces higher quality images with lower FID scores and better content preservation. Our work demonstrates that class-aware diffusion-based style transfer effectively bridges the synthetic-to-real domain gap even with minimal target domain data, advancing robust perception systems for challenging real-world applications. The source code is available at: https://github.com/echigot/cactif.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces two novel techniques, CACTI and CACTIF, for synthetic-to-real domain adaptation in semantic segmentation, leveraging diffusion models for style transfer. CACTI (Class-wise Adaptive Instance Normalization and Cross-Attention) applies statistical normalization selectively based on semantic classes, while CACTIF extends CACTI by selectively filtering cross-attention maps based on feature similarity to prevent artifacts. The methods aim to improve the performance of vision models trained on synthetic data by transferring style characteristics while preserving semantic boundaries and structural coherence. Experiments using GTA5 as the source domain and Cityscapes/ACDC as target domains demonstrate that the proposed approach generates higher quality images with lower FID scores and better content preservation, leading to improved semantic segmentation performance, especially in adverse weather conditions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces two novel techniques (CACTI and CACTIF) that refine diffusion-based style transfer for domain adaptation. The concept of class-wise adaptive instance normalization within a diffusion model context is relatively novel, addressing the limitations of global style transfer methods. The selective attention filtering mechanism, CACTIF, builds upon CACTI to further reduce artifacts. The approach of using feature similarity for filtering cross-attention maps is an improvement over previous cross-attention-based style transfer methods, specifically for domain adaptation.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in computer vision: the performance degradation of models trained on synthetic data when applied to real-world images due to the domain gap. By leveraging diffusion models for style transfer in a semantically aware manner, the paper provides a valuable solution for generating more realistic training data and bridging this gap. The focus on few-shot adaptation, where only limited target domain data is available, makes the approach particularly relevant in practical scenarios. The improved semantic segmentation performance, especially in adverse conditions like fog, rain, and snow, indicates the potential of the proposed method for real-world applications such as autonomous driving. The ablation study provided highlights the contributions of each stage of the proposed method.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Effective artifact reduction:** The proposed methods significantly reduce artifacts and improve visual coherence compared to existing style transfer techniques, as demonstrated in both qualitative and quantitative results.
    *   **Improved semantic segmentation performance:** The generated datasets lead to significant improvements in semantic segmentation performance, especially in adverse weather conditions, demonstrating the practical utility of the approach.
    *   **Focus on few-shot adaptation:**  Addressing the limited data setting increases the approach's practical relevance.
    *   **Class-aware style transfer:** Ensures the styles are transferred appropriately between the semantic regions.
    *   **Content Preservation:** The structure and semantic content of the original image is preserved through applying selective filtering.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational cost:**  Diffusion models are known for their computational intensity. Although the paper skips denoising steps, a more in-depth discussion of the computational efficiency of CACTI and CACTIF compared to alternative domain adaptation techniques would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited dataset variety for style transfer:** The style transfer is based on one image for each style. How the model will cope with a larger diversity of styles may need further examination.
    *   **Parameter sensitivity:** The performance is dependent on hyperparameter selection (e.g. the percentile parameter *p*). Further research on how these parameters should be chosen when dealing with a larger variety of styles and datasets would be helpful.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence future research in domain adaptation and style transfer, particularly for tasks where semantic consistency is crucial. The proposed techniques could be integrated into existing pipelines for data augmentation and model training, leading to more robust and reliable vision systems.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**  The paper makes a significant contribution by introducing semantically aware style transfer methods for domain adaptation using diffusion models. The improvements in image quality, content preservation, and semantic segmentation performance justify a strong score. While the computational cost and parameter sensitivity are potential limitations, the overall novelty, significance, and potential influence of the work warrant a score of 8. The score reflects the solid technical contribution and demonstrated practical improvements while acknowledging the room for further exploration and optimization.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### A collaborative constrained graph diffusion model for the generation of realistic synthetic molecules
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16365v1)
- **Authors**: Manuel Ruiz-Botella, Marta Sales-Pardo, Roger Guimerà
- **Abstract**: Developing new molecular compounds is crucial to address pressing challenges, from health to environmental sustainability. However, exploring the molecular space to discover new molecules is difficult due to the vastness of the space. Here we introduce CoCoGraph, a collaborative and constrained graph diffusion model capable of generating molecules that are guaranteed to be chemically valid. Thanks to the constraints built into the model and to the collaborative mechanism, CoCoGraph outperforms state-of-the-art approaches on standard benchmarks while requiring up to an order of magnitude fewer parameters. Analysis of 36 chemical properties also demonstrates that CoCoGraph generates molecules with distributions more closely matching real molecules than current models. Leveraging the model's efficiency, we created a database of 8.2M million synthetically generated molecules and conducted a Turing-like test with organic chemistry experts to further assess the plausibility of the generated molecules, and potential biases and limitations of CoCoGraph.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CoCoGraph, a novel collaborative constrained graph diffusion model for generating realistic synthetic molecules.  CoCoGraph uses a discrete diffusion process based on double edge swapping, which ensures that all generated molecules are chemically valid by construction.  A collaborative mechanism involves two models: a diffusion model that predicts the double edge swap to revert and a time model that predicts the diffusion time step, informing the diffusion model about its progress. This collaborative approach results in a model that outperforms state-of-the-art generative models on standard benchmarks while using significantly fewer parameters. The authors generate a large database of synthetic molecules and perform a Turing-like test, where organic chemistry experts try to distinguish between real and generated molecules, demonstrating the plausibility of the generated compounds.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary source of novelty lies in the *collaborative constrained graph diffusion framework*.  While diffusion models and graph neural networks have been used for molecule generation before, the combination of constrained double-edge swapping (to ensure chemical validity) with a collaborative time model is a unique architectural contribution. This also stands apart from recent constraint-aware diffusion models like Construct36. By imposing valence constraints directly in the process the model is designed to produce valid molecules, while existing models have to learn these rules.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Performance Improvement:**  The paper demonstrates that CoCoGraph achieves 100% chemical validity and outperforms existing methods (JTVAE and DiGress) on standard benchmarks like GuacaMol, achieving closer distributions of relevant molecular properties to real molecules. The improvement in KL divergence is statistically significant.
    *   **Efficiency:** CoCoGraph achieves these results with an order of magnitude fewer parameters than existing models, potentially lowering the computational barrier for molecular generation.
    *   **Scalability:** This lower computational footprint allowed for the creation of a large (8.2M) synthetic molecule database, useful for exploring new regions of the chemical space.
    *   **Plausibility:** The Turing-like test provides valuable evidence of the realism of the generated molecules, suggesting they closely mimic the structural characteristics of real molecules.
    *   **Limitations addressed:** Addresses issues of chemical validity and generalization in current generative molecule models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Double Edge Swapping Complexity:** As the authors acknowledge, the double edge swapping process has a computational complexity of *O(n⁴)* where *n* is number of atoms. Although mitigations are mentioned, this can be a limiting factor for very large molecules.
    *   **Molecular Formula Constraint:** The model maintains the same molecular formula throughout the diffusion process. This is a fundamental design decision and potentially limits its application in scenarios where the formula itself needs to be generated. A seeding algorithm for formula generation is suggested as a potential fix, but this adds complexity.
    *   **Expert Performance Baseline:** While experts could not reliably discriminate real molecules from CoCoGraph generated molecules, the performance against random guessing was close. This suggests that the molecules are plausible, but are there situations where performance is lower/random? Is this test adequate to ensure the general quality of the model?
    *   **Lack of Real World Validation:** The improved property matching and plausible molecular structure results from the Turing test are promising, but the paper does not provide validation of *real-world utility* – e.g., showing that molecules generated by CoCoGraph can be synthesized and display desired properties, or can be used to identify novel leads for specific targets.
    *   **Limited scope of properties**: The choice of 36 tested properties, although diverse, may be insufficient to capture every potentially relevant aspect of molecules' similarity to real ones.

*   **Potential Impact:** The method presents a significant advance in generative molecular modelling. The improved efficiency and plausibility of generated molecules should result in models with the ability to be more readily employed in a variety of research areas. The public release of a large, novel, chemically-validated molecule database is also a contribution in itself.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the novelty in the collaborative constrained diffusion framework, the demonstrated performance improvements, and the generation of a valuable database, CoCoGraph represents a significant advancement in the field of generative molecular modeling. While some limitations exist (especially related to computational complexity, and real world validation), the benefits are clearly articulated and strongly supported by experimental results. Therefore a score of **8** is warranted. The score is less than a 9 because there is no "real world validation" and the expert's difficulty in the Turing test was only slightly above random.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### ReCopilot: Reverse Engineering Copilot in Binary Analysis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16366v1)
- **Authors**: Guoqiang Chen, Huiqi Sun, Daguang Liu, Zhiqi Wang, Qiang Wang, Bin Yin, Lu Liu, Lingyun Ying
- **Abstract**: Binary analysis plays a pivotal role in security domains such as malware detection and vulnerability discovery, yet it remains labor-intensive and heavily reliant on expert knowledge. General-purpose large language models (LLMs) perform well in programming analysis on source code, while binaryspecific LLMs are underexplored. In this work, we present ReCopilot, an expert LLM designed for binary analysis tasks. ReCopilot integrates binary code knowledge through a meticulously constructed dataset, encompassing continue pretraining (CPT), supervised fine-tuning (SFT), and direct preference optimization (DPO) stages. It leverages variable data flow and call graph to enhance context awareness and employs test-time scaling to improve reasoning capabilities. Evaluations on a comprehensive binary analysis benchmark demonstrate that ReCopilot achieves state-of-the-art performance in tasks such as function name recovery and variable type inference on the decompiled pseudo code, outperforming both existing tools and LLMs by 13%. Our findings highlight the effectiveness of domain-specific training and context enhancement, while also revealing challenges in building super long chain-of-thought. ReCopilot represents a significant step toward automating binary analysis with interpretable and scalable AI assistance in this domain.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces ReCopilot, an expert large language model (LLM) specifically designed for binary analysis tasks.  The authors address the current limitations of general-purpose LLMs in this domain, which require domain-specific knowledge. ReCopilot is trained using a multi-stage approach: continued pre-training (CPT) on binary-related data, supervised fine-tuning (SFT) with a diverse dataset generated through a novel generator-discriminator framework incorporating chain-of-thought reasoning, and direct preference optimization (DPO) for format alignment. The paper also highlights a context enhancement technique leveraging static program analysis to extract relevant information (call chains and data flow) from the decompiled code to improve the model's awareness.  Evaluations on a comprehensive binary analysis benchmark demonstrate ReCopilot's superior performance compared to existing tools and general LLMs in tasks such as function name recovery, variable type inference, and decompilation.  The authors release a demo of ReCopilot to the security community.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper demonstrates a valuable contribution by focusing on a domain-specific LLM for binary analysis, an area where general LLMs often fall short. The novelty lies in the systematic approach to building this expert model, involving:

    *   *Targeted Dataset Construction*:  The creation of large-scale, high-quality binary analysis datasets for CPT, SFT, and DPO is a significant effort and a crucial step towards building effective domain-specific LLMs. The generator-discriminator framework for automatically generating SFT data with chain-of-thought reasoning is particularly innovative.
    *   *Context Enhancement Techniques*: Leveraging static program analysis for context enrichment (call chains and data flow) is a practical approach to improve the model's understanding of the binary code, something not always present in generalist models.
    *   *Multi-stage Training*: The combination of CPT, SFT, and DPO is a proven approach, but the authors do a good job of adapting and optimizing it for this particular problem domain.

*   **Significance:** Binary analysis is a critical area in cybersecurity. By automating and enhancing this process, ReCopilot has the potential to:

    *   *Reduce manual effort*:  Binary analysis is labor-intensive and requires expert knowledge. ReCopilot aims to ease this burden.
    *   *Improve accuracy*: The results demonstrate improved accuracy over existing tools, leading to better vulnerability detection and malware analysis.
    *   *Democratize access*: Making AI assistance available could enable more security professionals to perform binary analysis effectively.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   *Comprehensive evaluation*: The benchmark covers a range of tasks and compares ReCopilot against several baselines, including advanced general LLMs, providing a strong basis for assessing its performance.
    *   *Detailed methodology*: The paper provides thorough details on the dataset creation, training procedure, and context enhancement techniques, facilitating reproducibility and further research.
    *   *Public availability*:  Releasing a demo of ReCopilot promotes collaboration and accelerates research in this area.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   *Limited Task Focus:* While the benchmark includes multiple tasks, the paper acknowledges that ReCopilot currently focuses on fundamental decompilation-related tasks.  Higher-level analyses such as complex vulnerability discovery or exploit generation remain areas for future development.
    *   *Reliance on Pseudo Code:* ReCopilot operates primarily on decompiled pseudo code.  This can limit its applicability to scenarios where decompilation is unavailable or yields poor results (e.g., obfuscated binaries).
    *   *General Capability Compromise:* The paper acknowledges and quantifies the trade-off between domain-specific performance and general capabilities, as shown by the diminished performance of the fully-trained model on general LLM benchmarks. This is an expected result but illustrates the limitations.
    *   *Super-CoT Effectiveness:* The ablation study suggests that the gains from Super-CoT were not significant. This could be due to dataset imbalance or the limitations of directly transferring reasoning abilities without further refinement for more stable performance in deep reasoning.
    *   *Potential for Bias:* As with any LLM, ReCopilot is susceptible to biases present in the training data. This could lead to inaccurate or unfair analysis in certain scenarios.

*   **Impact:**  ReCopilot represents a step forward in automating binary analysis and shows the potential of expert LLMs in this field.  It provides a strong foundation for future research exploring more advanced analysis techniques, improved reasoning capabilities, and adaptation to other binary representations.

*  **Minor Weakness**: The paper's reliance on a commercial decompiler (IDA Pro) for the runtime environment could impede full replicability and accessibility of the benchmark.
**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

ReCopilot is a solid and innovative contribution to automated binary analysis. It has clear practical applications, demonstrates impressive performance improvements, and offers a well-documented methodology. While the paper has limitations, particularly regarding the restricted task focus, dependence on decompilation, the limited effectiveness of Super-CoT, and potential biases, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses. These limitations are clearly outlined, and the authors suggest potential directions for future research. The release of the ReCopilot demo further enhances the paper's impact by enabling wider use and exploration by the security community.

The score of 7 reflects that the work makes a substantial contribution, but has room for further refinement and broader application.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Chain-of-Thought Poisoning Attacks against R1-based Retrieval-Augmented Generation Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16367v1)
- **Authors**: Hongru Song, Yu-an Liu, Ruqing Zhang, Jiafeng Guo, Yixing Fan
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems can effectively mitigate the hallucination problem of large language models (LLMs),but they also possess inherent vulnerabilities. Identifying these weaknesses before the large-scale real-world deployment of RAG systems is of great importance, as it lays the foundation for building more secure and robust RAG systems in the future. Existing adversarial attack methods typically exploit knowledge base poisoning to probe the vulnerabilities of RAG systems, which can effectively deceive standard RAG models. However, with the rapid advancement of deep reasoning capabilities in modern LLMs, previous approaches that merely inject incorrect knowledge are inadequate when attacking RAG systems equipped with deep reasoning abilities. Inspired by the deep thinking capabilities of LLMs, this paper extracts reasoning process templates from R1-based RAG systems, uses these templates to wrap erroneous knowledge into adversarial documents, and injects them into the knowledge base to attack RAG systems. The key idea of our approach is that adversarial documents, by simulating the chain-of-thought patterns aligned with the model's training signals, may be misinterpreted by the model as authentic historical reasoning processes, thus increasing their likelihood of being referenced. Experiments conducted on the MS MARCO passage ranking dataset demonstrate the effectiveness of our proposed method.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Chain-of-Thought Poisoning Attacks against R1-based Retrieval-Augmented Generation Systems":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel attack method against Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems, specifically those leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) with deep reasoning capabilities (R1-based RAG).  Existing knowledge base poisoning attacks are often ineffective against these systems because they are better at filtering out incorrect information. The proposed method, called "Chain-of-Thought Poisoning," exploits the structured reasoning process that R1-based RAG systems exhibit. It extracts the common reasoning format from the RAG's responses, then uses this template to create adversarial documents containing erroneous knowledge wrapped in plausible reasoning chains. These documents, when injected into the knowledge base, are more likely to be retrieved and accepted by the LLM, leading to manipulated responses. The paper validates this approach through experiments on the MS MARCO dataset, demonstrating improved attack success rates compared to baseline methods, especially against R1-based RAG systems.  They also conduct human evaluations to confirm the naturalness of the crafted adversarial documents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of targeting the reasoning chain itself, rather than simply injecting incorrect facts, is reasonably novel.  Previous work on adversarial attacks against RAG systems mostly focused on knowledge base poisoning or manipulating ranking. Exploiting the "chain-of-thought" behavior of advanced LLMs within a RAG framework to craft more effective attacks is a distinct contribution. The idea of extracting the reasoning format and using it as a template is clever.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in highlighting a new vulnerability in RAG systems, particularly those employing deep reasoning LLMs.  As RAG systems become more prevalent, understanding their weaknesses is crucial for building more robust systems.  The study demonstrates that even systems designed to mitigate hallucinations can be susceptible to attacks that target their reasoning process.  The ability to not only mislead the model but also to make the attack appear natural is an important finding.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method and its motivation.
    *   The experimental setup is relatively thorough, including comparisons to relevant baselines and evaluations on different types of RAG systems.
    *   The use of human evaluators to assess the naturalness of the attack is a strong point, adding credibility to the findings.
    *   The parameter scaling experiments provide insights into the relationship between model size and attack effectiveness.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experimental evaluation is limited to the MS MARCO dataset. While this is a standard benchmark, it would be beneficial to test the approach on other datasets and in more real-world scenarios.
    *   The paper doesn't deeply explore potential defenses against the proposed attack.  While identifying vulnerabilities is important, suggesting mitigation strategies would enhance the paper's impact.
    * The improvement in ASR between PoisonedRAG and the proposed method is small.
    *   While the concept is relatively novel, the practical implementation relies heavily on prompting LLMs. The robustness of the attack may vary depending on the specific LLMs used and the prompt engineering.
    *   The analysis focuses mostly on attack effectiveness.  A more detailed analysis of why the attack works and how it affects the LLM's reasoning process would be valuable.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a tangible contribution by identifying and exploiting a previously underexplored vulnerability in R1-based RAG systems. The concept of "chain-of-thought poisoning" is creative and well-executed. However, the relatively modest performance gains over the "PoisonedRAG" baseline, the narrow scope of the experimental evaluation, and the lack of a discussion on potential defenses limit the overall impact. The dependence on prompt engineering also introduces a degree of uncertainty regarding the generalizability of the results.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 6/10

### SATURN: SAT-based Reinforcement Learning to Unleash Language Model Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16368v1)
- **Authors**: Huanyu Liu, Jia Li, Hao Zhu, Kechi Zhang, Yihong Dong, Ge Li
- **Abstract**: How to design reinforcement learning (RL) tasks that effectively unleash the reasoning capability of large language models (LLMs) remains an open question. Existing RL tasks (e.g., math, programming, and constructing reasoning tasks) suffer from three key limitations: (1) Scalability. They rely heavily on human annotation or expensive LLM synthesis to generate sufficient training data. (2) Verifiability. LLMs' outputs are hard to verify automatically and reliably. (3) Controllable Difficulty. Most tasks lack fine-grained difficulty control, making it hard to train LLMs to develop reasoning ability from easy to hard. To address these limitations, we propose Saturn, a SAT-based RL framework that uses Boolean Satisfiability (SAT) problems to train and evaluate LLM reasoning. Saturn enables scalable task construction, rule-based verification, and precise difficulty control. Saturn designs a curriculum learning pipeline that continuously improves LLMs' reasoning capability by constructing SAT tasks of increasing difficulty and training LLMs from easy to hard. To ensure stable training, we design a principled mechanism to control difficulty transitions. We introduce Saturn-2.6k, a dataset of 2,660 SAT problems with varying difficulty. It supports the evaluation of how LLM reasoning changes with problem difficulty. We apply Saturn to DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen and obtain Saturn-1.5B and Saturn-7B. We achieve several notable results: (1) On SAT problems, Saturn-1.5B and Saturn-7B achieve average pass@3 improvements of +14.0 and +28.1, respectively. (2) On math and programming tasks, Saturn-1.5B and Saturn-7B improve average scores by +4.9 and +1.8 on benchmarks (e.g., AIME, LiveCodeBench). (3) Compared to the state-of-the-art (SOTA) approach in constructing RL tasks, Saturn achieves further improvements of +8.8%. We release the source code, data, and models to support future research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SATURN paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SATURN, a reinforcement learning (RL) framework designed to enhance the reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). SATURN leverages Boolean Satisfiability (SAT) problems as the core RL task.  It addresses limitations of existing RL training tasks by offering:

*   **Scalability:**  SAT problems can be generated programmatically at scale, removing the need for human annotation or expensive LLM synthesis.
*   **Verifiability:** The correctness of solutions can be easily verified using established SAT solvers, ensuring unambiguous rewards.
*   **Controllable Difficulty:**  The difficulty of SAT instances can be precisely adjusted by varying parameters like the number of variables and clauses, enabling curriculum learning.

SATURN employs a curriculum learning approach, progressively training LLMs on SAT tasks of increasing difficulty. To maintain stability, the framework includes a mechanism for controlling difficulty transitions based on LLM performance. The authors introduce SATURN-2.6k, a dataset of SAT problems with varying difficulty levels, and apply SATURN to DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen models, resulting in SATURN-1.5B and SATURN-7B.  Experimental results demonstrate improvements in SAT solving, generalization to math and programming tasks, and enhanced self-verification capabilities. The authors release their code, data, and models to support future research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific application of SAT problems within an RL curriculum learning framework for LLM reasoning. While RL for LLMs and SAT-based reasoning have been explored previously, the authors combine these elements in a structured and scalable manner, targeting the specific challenge of controllable difficulty. The design of the curriculum with difficulty control and the engineering of the SAT instance generator provide demonstrable novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its potential to address the key bottlenecks in training LLMs for complex reasoning.  The ability to scalably generate training data, reliably verify results, and precisely control difficulty are critical for fostering progressive learning. The experiments show promising results, including transfer learning to math and programming problems and a comparison against SOTA RL methods further highlighting it's significance. Moreover, The open-sourcing of code, data, and models greatly enhances its potential impact by enabling further exploration by other researchers.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-Defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing RL tasks for LLM reasoning.
    *   **Principled Approach:** SATURN's design is based on well-established principles in SAT solving and curriculum learning.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating improvements in both SAT solving and transfer learning.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough evaluation, comparing against strong baselines and exploring the impact of different training settings.
    *   **Open-Source Release:** The availability of code, data, and models promotes reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The paper primarily focuses on relatively small language models (1.5B and 7B parameters). It could benefit from showcasing the effectiveness of SATURN on larger, more powerful models.
    *   **Task Specificity:** SATURN's reliance on SAT problems could potentially limit its broader applicability to other types of reasoning tasks. It may not be a "one-size-fits-all" solution.
    *   **Complexity:** The setup and training involved in SATURN requires substantial computational resources. It might be computationally expensive for researchers with fewer resources.
    *   **Lack of theoretical explanation**: While the difficulty estimation is derived analytically, there isn't a strong theoretical justification for the choice of the formula (e.g., showing it's correlated with some measure of the model's internal state).

*   **Potential Influence:** SATURN could influence the field by:
    *   Inspiring new RL frameworks for LLM reasoning based on symbolic domains.
    *   Providing a scalable and verifiable alternative to existing RL tasks.
    *   Encouraging research on curriculum learning and difficulty control for LLMs.
    *   Serving as a benchmark for evaluating reasoning capabilities.

*   **Justification of Score:**
    I assign the paper a score of 8.  It presents a novel and well-engineered RL framework that addresses key limitations in training LLMs for reasoning. The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating improvements in SAT solving and transfer learning. The open-source release further enhances its potential impact. However, the limited scope of the experiments (primarily small models, a specific class of tasks) and the lack of strong theoretical support moderate the score. It's a strong and valuable contribution, but further work is needed to fully assess its generalizability and scalability.
