- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Locate-then-Merge: Neuron-Level Parameter Fusion for Mitigating Catastrophic Forgetting in Multimodal LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16703v1)
- **Authors**: Zeping Yu, Sophia Ananiadou
- **Abstract**: Although multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have achieved impressive performance, the multimodal instruction tuning stage often causes catastrophic forgetting of the base LLM's language ability, even in strong models like Llama3. To address this, we propose Locate-then-Merge, a training-free parameter fusion framework that first locates important parameters and then selectively merges them. We further introduce Neuron-Fusion, a neuron-level strategy that preserves the influence of neurons with large parameter shifts--neurons likely responsible for newly acquired visual capabilities--while attenuating the influence of neurons with smaller changes that likely encode general-purpose language skills. This design enables better retention of visual adaptation while mitigating language degradation. Experiments on 13 benchmarks across both language and visual tasks show that Neuron-Fusion consistently outperforms existing model merging methods. Further analysis reveals that our method effectively reduces context hallucination in generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Locate-then-Merge: Neuron-Level Parameter Fusion for Mitigating Catastrophic Forgetting in Multimodal LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of catastrophic forgetting in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) when adapting pre-trained LLMs to incorporate visual information. The authors propose a training-free parameter fusion framework called "Locate-then-Merge," which first identifies important parameters and then selectively merges them. Specifically, they introduce "Neuron-Fusion," a neuron-level strategy that focuses on preserving neurons with large parameter shifts (assumed to be responsible for newly acquired visual capabilities) while attenuating neurons with smaller changes (associated with general language skills). Experiments on a suite of language and visual benchmarks demonstrate that Neuron-Fusion outperforms existing model merging techniques and effectively reduces context hallucination in generated text.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its targeted approach to model merging at the neuron level, specifically designed to address catastrophic forgetting in *multimodal* LLMs. While model merging techniques themselves are not new, the focus on neuron-level selection based on parameter change magnitude and the distinction between preserving visual capabilities and suppressing language degradation is a worthwhile contribution. The Locate-then-Merge framework is a useful abstraction for thinking about existing model merging techniques.  However, it does not introduce new mathematical formulations, but re-frames existing methods.

*   **Significance:** The problem of catastrophic forgetting is a significant obstacle in the development of robust and generalizable MLLMs. The paper provides a practical and training-free solution that can potentially alleviate this issue, making it easier to adapt LLMs for multimodal tasks without sacrificing language understanding and reasoning abilities. The empirical results seem strong, showing consistent improvements across multiple benchmarks. Furthermore, the analysis of failure cases (Not-Known and Context-Hallucination) provides valuable insights into the mechanisms by which Neuron-Fusion achieves its benefits.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated approach.
    *   Neuron-Fusion demonstrates a targeted approach by focusing on neuron-level parameter changes.
    *   Extensive experimental evaluation across a range of language and vision benchmarks.
    *   Analysis of failure cases leading to concrete conclusions about the nature of the approach.
    *   The Locate-then-Merge is a helpful abstraction/generalization.
    *   The experiments seem strong, using popular LLMs (Llama and Mistral).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The assumption that large parameter changes directly correspond to important visual neurons could be an oversimplification. Further analysis into the specific functions learned by these neurons would strengthen this claim.
    *   The method's effectiveness may be highly dependent on the specific architecture and training regime of the base LLM and MLLM. The paper's generalizability to other types of MLLMs or training paradigms is not fully explored.
    *   While the Locate-then-Merge framework is useful for conceptualizing existing methods, it doesn't necessarily lead to the creation of radically new merging techniques.  It's more of a reframing than a breakthrough.
    *   The ablation study could be more comprehensive. It primarily focuses on varying K and M. Exploring the interaction between different components of Neuron-Fusion (e.g., varying both Neuron-Suppress and Neuron-Restore strategies simultaneously) would provide a more complete understanding.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research in model merging, particularly for multimodal models. The neuron-level approach and the focus on mitigating catastrophic forgetting may inspire other researchers to explore more targeted and adaptive fusion strategies.  The framework of Locate-then-Merge could prove to be a useful pedagogical tool.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the paper presents a valuable and well-executed approach, its novelty is somewhat incremental, and its generalizability has not been fully established. The idea of neuron-level selection is interesting but requires more in-depth investigation. The approach, while strong in its results, doesn't seem like a radical departure.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Training Long-Context LLMs Efficiently via Chunk-wise Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16710v1)
- **Authors**: Wenhao Li, Yuxin Zhang, Gen Luo, Daohai Yu, Rongrong Ji
- **Abstract**: While long-context large language models (LLMs) exhibit remarkable document processing capabilities, their prohibitively high training costs often hinder customized applications. To mitigate this issue, we propose \textit{Sequential Chunk-wise Optimization} (SeCO), a memory-efficient training paradigm that partitions lengthy inputs into manageable chunks. Each chunk independently constructs its computational graph and performs localized backpropagation, ensuring that only one chunk's forward activations are stored in memory. Building on SeCO, we further introduce \textit{Sparse Chunk-wise Optimization} (SpaCO), which reduces computational overhead by selectively propagating gradients to specific chunks and incorporates a carefully designed compensation factor to ensure unbiased gradient estimation. SpaCO decouples the computational cost of backpropagation from the context length, enabling training time to gradually converge to inference time as sequences become longer. Implemented as lightweight training wrappers, both SeCO and SpaCO offer substantial practical benefits. For example, when fine-tuning an 8B model with LoRA on a single RTX 3090 GPU, SeCO expands maximum sequence length from 1K to 16K tokens, while SpaCO demonstrates accelerated training speed -- achieving up to 3x faster than SeCO under the same experimental setup. These innovations provide new insights into optimizing long-context models, making them more accessible for practical applications. We have open-sourced the code at \href{https://github.com/wenhaoli-xmu/seco}{here}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Sequential Chunk-wise Optimization (SeCO) and Sparse Chunk-wise Optimization (SpaCO) as memory and computation-efficient training methods for long-context large language models (LLMs). SeCO addresses the memory constraints of fine-tuning by partitioning long input sequences into manageable chunks and applying gradient checkpointing along the sequence dimension, thereby significantly reducing memory consumption without compromising gradient accuracy. SpaCO builds upon SeCO by introducing sparsification in backpropagation, selectively propagating gradients through a subset of chunks to reduce computational overhead. It uses a theoretically grounded compensation factor to ensure unbiased gradient estimation despite the sparsification. Experiments demonstrate that SeCO expands the trainable sequence length on limited hardware, while SpaCO accelerates training, converging faster as sequence length increases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of chunk-wise optimization isn't entirely new, given the prevalence of techniques like chunking in inference and even some prior work on gradient checkpointing with fixed partitioning.  However, the *application* of gradient checkpointing *along the sequence dimension* and the development of SpaCO with its theoretical grounding for unbiased sparse gradient estimation show significant novelty. The compensation factor design to address the bias introduced by sparse backpropagation is a valuable contribution. The architecture insights presented, are novel. This provides SpaCO the ability to reduce memory consumption and provide speed while maintaining satisfactory performance.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical and significant bottleneck in LLM research and development: the high cost of training long-context models.  By providing methods that demonstrably reduce memory and computation requirements on consumer hardware (e.g., a single RTX 3090), the work potentially democratizes access to long-context fine-tuning. The experimental results support the effectiveness of both SeCO and SpaCO in different scenarios. Although, the improvement and the effectiveness is heavily dependant on hyperparameter tuning.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a critical problem:**  Long-context LLMs are important, but resource-intensive.
    *   **Practical and hardware-aware:** The focus on efficiency on widely available consumer hardware is important for accessibility.
    *   **Sound theoretical foundation:** SpaCO's design, especially the compensation factor, is backed by theoretical analysis to mitigate bias.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The paper includes experiments demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental improvements:** The methods build upon existing techniques, and the performance gains, while significant, might not be revolutionary.
    *   **Complexity:** Implementing SeCO and SpaCO might require significant code modifications and a deep understanding of the underlying framework. The implementations requires some expertise with gradient back propogation.
    *   **Limited evaluation diversity:** While the experiments cover the core claims, more diverse datasets and model sizes would further strengthen the results.

*   **Potential Impact:** If widely adopted, SeCO and SpaCO could make long-context LLMs more accessible to researchers and practitioners with limited resources. This could, in turn, accelerate innovation in applications that benefit from processing long documents. The methods introduced provide novel architecture insights.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper doesn't introduce a completely revolutionary paradigm shift, it offers a valuable and practical set of techniques for addressing a critical bottleneck in LLM development. The combination of chunk-wise gradient checkpointing and the theoretically grounded sparse gradient estimation in SpaCO demonstrates significant novelty and practical utility.  The focus on consumer hardware makes the work more accessible and potentially impactful. However, the incremental nature of the improvements and the potential implementation complexity prevent a higher score.

### Breaking mBad! Supervised Fine-tuning for Cross-Lingual Detoxification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16722v1)
- **Authors**: Himanshu Beniwal, Youngwoo Kim, Maarten Sap, Soham Dan, Thomas Hartvigsen
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) become increasingly prevalent in global applications, ensuring that they are toxicity-free across diverse linguistic contexts remains a critical challenge. We explore "Cross-lingual Detoxification", a cross-lingual paradigm that mitigates toxicity, enabling detoxification capabilities to transfer between high and low-resource languages across different script families. We analyze cross-lingual detoxification's effectiveness through 504 extensive settings to evaluate toxicity reduction in cross-distribution settings with limited data and investigate how mitigation impacts model performance on non-toxic tasks, revealing trade-offs between safety and knowledge preservation. Our code and dataset are publicly available at https://github.com/himanshubeniwal/Breaking-mBad.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize the paper and provide a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance with a justified score.

**Summary:**

The paper "Breaking mBad! Supervised Fine-tuning for Cross-Lingual Detoxification" explores the challenge of mitigating toxicity in multilingual large language models (m-LLMs).  The authors introduce and investigate "Cross-lingual Detoxification" (X-DET), a methodology where a language model is detoxified in a source language, and the effectiveness of this detoxification is evaluated across nine target languages. The study utilizes parallel toxic-neutral sentence pairs for supervised fine-tuning. The paper analyzes the effectiveness of cross-lingual detoxification across different language families, under limited data conditions, and investigates the trade-offs between toxicity reduction and knowledge/performance preservation on non-toxic tasks. The authors conduct a comprehensive set of experiments (504 settings) and present empirical findings demonstrating the potential for effective cross-lingual toxicity transfer, especially among languages with similar linguistic properties (e.g., Indo-European).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Important Problem:** The paper addresses a crucial and timely issue: the propagation of harmful content by m-LLMs across different linguistic contexts. The global deployment of these models necessitates addressing toxicity in various languages and cultural contexts, making the problem significant.
    *   **Comprehensive Experimentation:** The extensive experimental setup (504 configurations) is a significant strength. Exploring multiple language pairs, learning strategies (Zero-shot, cross-lingual finetuning etc), and models adds substantial weight to the findings. The focus on limited data scenarios is also practically relevant.
    *   **Empirical Analysis of Cross-Lingual Transfer:** The paper provides empirical evidence suggesting that cross-lingual detoxification can be effective. The analysis of how linguistic properties influence toxicity transfer (finding that Indo-European languages show better transfer than others) is insightful.
    *   **Publicly Available Resources:** Making the code and dataset publicly available enhances reproducibility and facilitates future research in this area.
    *  **Demonstrates the problem effectively**: Showing that LLMs can produce offensive completions without cross-lingual detoxification.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Languages:** While the paper covers nine languages, expanding the language set further would enhance the generalizability of the findings. Specifically, inclusion of more languages from underrepresented script families would be beneficial.
    *   **Reliance on Traditional Fine-tuning:** The choice of traditional fine-tuning, while justified by the focus on limited data, neglects the exploration of more recent and potentially effective techniques like chain-of-thought prompting, Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), or model editing. A comparison with or investigation of these alternatives could significantly strengthen the paper.
    *   **Dependence on Perspective API:** Relying solely on the Perspective API for toxicity evaluation is a limitation. While it provides a standardized approach, it might not capture the nuances of toxicity across all languages, especially within specific cultural contexts. The API's biases may also affect the results. Exploring other toxicity assessment tools or human evaluation could improve the robustness of the findings.
    *   **Novelty not breakthrough**: While the topic is important and the experimentation is extensive, the method itself is relatively straightforward (supervised fine-tuning). The value primarily lies in the systematic empirical investigation of the method in a cross-lingual setting, rather than a novel algorithmic contribution.
    *  **Ethical concerns section is thin**: While the authors mention ethical considerations, a deeper discussion of the ethical implications of toxicity mitigation and the potential for unintended consequences (e.g., censorship, bias amplification) would be welcome.
    *  **Choice of Model not justified**: The justification of why they chose aya-expanse-8B, mT5-Large, and bloom-7B1 are not clearly justified.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Practical Implications:** The study offers practical insights for developers deploying m-LLMs in global settings. The findings can guide the development of strategies to mitigate toxicity in low-resource languages.
    *   **Foundation for Future Research:**  The work serves as a baseline and starting point for more sophisticated cross-lingual detoxification techniques. It highlights the importance of considering linguistic properties in cross-lingual transfer learning.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by systematically investigating cross-lingual detoxification. While the methodological novelty is limited and the dependence on a single toxicity API raises some concerns, the comprehensive experimental setup, empirical findings, and practical implications make the work valuable. The insights regarding linguistic properties and transferability, along with the public release of code and data, will likely influence future research in this area. However, I am hesitant to rate it very highly since it is not a breakthrough and the weaknesses are significant enough.

### Robust LLM Fingerprinting via Domain-Specific Watermarks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16723v1)
- **Authors**: Thibaud Gloaguen, Robin Staab, Nikola Jovanović, Martin Vechev
- **Abstract**: As open-source language models (OSMs) grow more capable and are widely shared and finetuned, ensuring model provenance, i.e., identifying the origin of a given model instance, has become an increasingly important issue. At the same time, existing backdoor-based model fingerprinting techniques often fall short of achieving key requirements of real-world model ownership detection. In this work, we build on the observation that while current open-source model watermarks fail to achieve reliable content traceability, they can be effectively adapted to address the challenge of model provenance. To this end, we introduce the concept of domain-specific watermarking for model fingerprinting. Rather than watermarking all generated content, we train the model to embed watermarks only within specified subdomains (e.g., particular languages or topics). This targeted approach ensures detection reliability, while improving watermark durability and quality under a range of real-world deployment settings. Our evaluations show that domain-specific watermarking enables model fingerprinting with strong statistical guarantees, controllable false positive rates, high detection power, and preserved generation quality. Moreover, we find that our fingerprints are inherently stealthy and naturally robust to real-world variability across deployment scenarios.
- **Summary**: The paper "Robust LLM Fingerprinting via Domain-Specific Watermarks" introduces a novel approach to model fingerprinting by adapting open-source language model (OSM) watermarks to domain-specific applications. Instead of embedding watermarks in all generated content, the model is trained to embed them only within specified subdomains (e.g., particular languages or topics). This targeted approach aims to improve detection reliability, watermark durability, and generation quality. The authors demonstrate that domain-specific watermarking enables model fingerprinting with strong statistical guarantees, controllable false positive rates, high detection power, preserved generation quality, inherent stealth, and robustness to real-world variability across deployment scenarios. They conduct experiments on LLAMA3.2-1B-INSTRUCT and QWEN2.5-7B-INSTRUCT finetuned on various domains, including Math, Health, and French, to showcase the effectiveness of their method.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of domain-specific watermarking for model fingerprinting is a valuable contribution. While generation-time watermarks have been explored, their direct application to model provenance is hampered by performance degradation and lack of durability. The paper addresses these limitations by restricting watermarking to specific domains, which allows for a more effective and stealthy fingerprinting approach. This is a solid conceptual step.

*   **Significance:** Model provenance is becoming an increasingly important issue with the proliferation of open-source LLMs. The paper tackles this problem by providing a practically viable, black-box fingerprinting technique that addresses the limitations of existing methods. It also has legal ramifications. While prior fingerprinting techniques often require direct model access or are easily detectable, the proposed domain-specific watermarking offers a better balance of robustness, stealthiness, and practicality. The study makes a clear case.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges in model fingerprinting and the shortcomings of existing methods.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** The domain-specific watermarking technique is well-defined and easy to understand.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are thorough and cover various aspects, including reliability, durability, robustness, and stealthiness.
    *   **Strong Statistical Guarantees:** The use of watermarks based on statistical hypotheses allows for strong guarantees regarding false positive rates.
    *   **Practical Considerations:** The paper addresses practical deployment scenarios and emphasizes the importance of stealthiness and robustness.
    *   Comprehensive additional experiments and ablations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Domain Selection Dependence:** The effectiveness of the method relies on choosing a domain suitable for watermarking. Low entropy domains (e.g., Math) prove to be more challenging. The dependence on a priori domain knowledge is somewhat limiting.
    *   **Domain Leakage:** As the authors show, domain leakage in the regularization dataset can significantly degrade the specificity of the watermark. Addressing this in real-world scenarios may require careful data curation.
    *   **Adversarial Finetuning:** While the paper considers finetuning as a form of model modification, it does not explore more sophisticated adversarial finetuning techniques specifically designed to remove watermarks. This is important.
    *   Although the work uses two strong language models for experimentation, the 1B parameter models show better success. There may be scaling challenges.
    *   The technique relies on a black-box setting, and it is unknown how domain-specific watermarks could be detected given some access to the model.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to influence future research on model fingerprinting and watermarking. The domain-specific approach provides a new perspective and addresses some of the critical limitations of existing methods. The paper could serve as a starting point for developing more robust and practical fingerprinting techniques for open-source LLMs. Additionally, it could contribute to establishing best practices for model provenance and licensing enforcement.

Score: 7.5

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and practically relevant contribution to the field of model fingerprinting. The domain-specific watermarking technique addresses key limitations of existing approaches, offering a better balance of reliability, stealthiness, and robustness. The thorough experimental evaluation provides strong evidence supporting the effectiveness of the method. While the dependence on domain selection and the potential for domain leakage are limitations, the paper offers a valuable starting point for future research. The high score reflects the paper's novel approach, practical implications, and potential influence on the field. The fact that black-box settings are evaluated, and that the work tries to improve on previous fingerprinting attacks, shows that the authors are really thinking about the broader impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Masked Conditioning for Deep Generative Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16725v1)
- **Authors**: Phillip Mueller, Jannik Wiese, Sebastian Mueller, Lars Mikelsons
- **Abstract**: Datasets in engineering domains are often small, sparsely labeled, and contain numerical as well as categorical conditions. Additionally. computational resources are typically limited in practical applications which hinders the adoption of generative models for engineering tasks. We introduce a novel masked-conditioning approach, that enables generative models to work with sparse, mixed-type data. We mask conditions during training to simulate sparse conditions at inference time. For this purpose, we explore the use of various sparsity schedules that show different strengths and weaknesses. In addition, we introduce a flexible embedding that deals with categorical as well as numerical conditions. We integrate our method into an efficient variational autoencoder as well as a latent diffusion model and demonstrate the applicability of our approach on two engineering-related datasets of 2D point clouds and images. Finally, we show that small models trained on limited data can be coupled with large pretrained foundation models to improve generation quality while retaining the controllability induced by our conditioning scheme.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel masked-conditioning approach for deep generative models (DGMs) to address challenges in engineering design tasks. These challenges often involve small, sparsely labeled datasets containing mixed data types (numerical and categorical) and limited computational resources. The authors propose masking conditions during training to simulate sparse conditions during inference, exploring different sparsity schedules. They also introduce a flexible embedding method to handle mixed data types. The approach is integrated into variational autoencoders (VAEs) and latent diffusion models (LDMs), demonstrating its applicability on 2D point clouds and image datasets related to engineering design. Furthermore, the authors explore combining small-scale DGMs trained on limited engineering data with large pre-trained models like Stable Diffusion to improve generation quality while maintaining controllability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper demonstrates a reasonable degree of novelty. Masking input conditions to generative models isn't entirely new, but the specific application to sparse and mixed-type engineering datasets, along with the detailed exploration of sparsity schedules and the integration with both VAEs and LDMs, adds a unique perspective. The exploration of combining smaller domain-specific models with larger pre-trained ones for refinement is a relevant and practical approach. The conditioning architecture and embedding scheme appear tailored and novel to the problem context.

**Significance:**

The significance of the paper is moderate. Engineering design often faces data limitations, making general-purpose DGMs difficult to directly apply. By addressing the challenges of sparse, mixed-type data, the paper contributes to making DGMs more accessible and useful in this domain. The experiments on relevant datasets (GeoBIKED and a proprietary vehicle dataset) provide practical insights. The method's ability to function with varying degrees of sparsity and the findings regarding dataset size thresholds are valuable. The combination with Stable Diffusion showcases a way to leverage existing resources for enhanced image realism.

**Strengths:**

*   **Practical focus:** Addresses a real-world problem in engineering design with data limitations.
*   **Comprehensive approach:** Explores different sparsity schedules and integrates with both VAEs and LDMs.
*   **Combination of smaller domain-specific models with larger pre-trained models:**  Offers a practical solution.
*   **Evaluations on relevant datasets:**  Provides practical insights into the method's performance.
*   The paper is well-written and relatively easy to understand.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited extrapolation capabilities:** The method may struggle with completely novel conditions.
*   **Dependency on pre-trained VAE:** The LDMs rely on a pre-trained VAE, which could limit performance or require finetuning that is complex.
*   **Lack of comparison against robust baselines for sparse data imputation.** The paper compares mainly against unconditioned methods or methods with full conditioning.  Including a comparison against existing techniques specifically tailored for handling missing data in VAEs/LDMs would have strengthened the evaluation.
*   The evaluation of the LDMs could have been more extensive. While qualitative results are presented, adding more quantitative metrics (e.g., Inception Score, FID) would provide a more rigorous comparison.
*   While the paper highlights reduced resource needs, a direct comparison of training time/costs against training a similar model without the masking approach would be valuable.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the application of DGMs in engineering design by providing a practical approach to handle sparse and mixed-type data. The combination of smaller and larger models could become a common strategy.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper has reasonable novelty and moderate significance. While the individual components of the approach (masking, VAEs, LDMs) aren't entirely new, the combination and adaptation to the engineering design context make a valuable contribution. The limitations concerning extrapolation and more extensive evaluation (including more direct quantitative comparisons) prevent a higher score.

### Forward-only Diffusion Probabilistic Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16733v1)
- **Authors**: Ziwei Luo, Fredrik K. Gustafsson, Jens Sjölund, Thomas B. Schön
- **Abstract**: This work presents a forward-only diffusion (FoD) approach for generative modelling. In contrast to traditional diffusion models that rely on a coupled forward-backward diffusion scheme, FoD directly learns data generation through a single forward diffusion process, yielding a simple yet efficient generative framework. The core of FoD is a state-dependent linear stochastic differential equation that involves a mean-reverting term in both the drift and diffusion functions. This mean-reversion property guarantees the convergence to clean data, naturally simulating a stochastic interpolation between source and target distributions. More importantly, FoD is analytically tractable and is trained using a simple stochastic flow matching objective, enabling a few-step non-Markov chain sampling during inference. The proposed FoD model, despite its simplicity, achieves competitive performance on various image-conditioned (e.g., image restoration) and unconditional generation tasks, demonstrating its effectiveness in generative modelling. Our code is available at https://github.com/Algolzw/FoD.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Forward-only Diffusion Probabilistic Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach called Forward-only Diffusion (FoD) for generative modeling.  Unlike traditional diffusion models that rely on a forward corruption process and a learned reverse process, FoD directly learns data generation using a single, analytically tractable forward diffusion process.  The key innovation is a state-dependent linear SDE incorporating a mean-reverting term in both drift and diffusion. This mean-reversion ensures convergence to clean data and facilitates stochastic interpolation between distributions. FoD is trained with a stochastic flow matching objective, allowing for efficient few-step non-Markov chain sampling.  The paper demonstrates competitive performance on image-conditioned (restoration) and unconditional generation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The central idea of a *forward-only* diffusion process is interesting and represents a significant departure from the standard forward-backward diffusion framework. The introduction of the state-dependent, mean-reverting SDE is the most novel aspect. It provides a mechanism for guaranteed convergence to clean data, which is not inherently present in flow matching or standard diffusion models. By framing this process with a stochastic interpolation between source and target distributions, a distinct advantage is achieved as there is no requirement for a corruption process of the original images.

**Significance:**

*   **Simplicity:**  The primary significance lies in the simplification of the generative modeling pipeline. Removing the need to learn a reverse process and training a single process could lead to faster training times and reduced computational overhead.
*   **Image-Conditioned Generation:** The approach is well-suited for image-conditioned generation tasks, where starting from a corrupted, but structurally informative, initial state is more desirable than starting from pure noise. The experiments on image restoration tasks support this claim.
*   **Analytical Tractability:** The analytical tractability of FoD enables efficient inference, specifically, the few-step sampling strategies. This is a substantial advantage compared to many diffusion models that require a large number of sampling steps to generate high-quality results.
*   **Extension of Flow Matching:** The paper correctly identifies FoD as a stochastic extension of flow matching. A limitation is, that the forward-only schemes lack the data corruption benefits of the forward-backward schemes. In the experiments however, the paper shows that FoD is competitive with forward-backward schemes and overcomes this limitation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Unconditional Generation:** The performance on unconditional image generation (CIFAR-10) is not state-of-the-art and lags behind the standard diffusion models. This suggests that the framework might be more suitable for conditional generation or restoration tasks. The paper acknowledges that achieving comparable performance on complex unconditional generation tasks is an area for future research.
*   **Experimental Scope:** While the paper covers a range of image restoration tasks, a broader evaluation on more diverse and challenging datasets would strengthen the claims.
*   **Ablation Studies:** While the paper includes limited ablation studies to highlight important design choices, a more systematic analysis (e.g., varying the mean-reverting strength) could provide a deeper understanding of the model's behavior.

**Impact:**

The potential impact of this work lies in its contribution to a new perspective on generative modeling, highlighting the possibility of simpler, more efficient architectures. The state-dependent mean-reverting diffusion process could influence future research in developing alternative generative frameworks, particularly for image-conditioned tasks. It also offers a novel perspective for those in flow matching, as the primary FoD model can be regarded as a stochastic extension of flow matching models.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel framework with strong potential for image-conditioned tasks. However, the weaker performance in unconditional generation and the need for more extensive experiments temper the overall assessment. The simplification and improved efficiency are significant, but require further exploration and validation on more complex datasets. The analytical tractability and efficient sampling are considerable advantages, warranting a higher score.

### Mitigating Fine-tuning Risks in LLMs via Safety-Aware Probing Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16737v1)
- **Authors**: Chengcan Wu, Zhixin Zhang, Zeming Wei, Yihao Zhang, Meng Sun
- **Abstract**: The significant progress of large language models (LLMs) has led to remarkable achievements across numerous applications. However, their ability to generate harmful content has sparked substantial safety concerns. Despite the implementation of safety alignment techniques during the pre-training phase, recent research indicates that fine-tuning LLMs on adversarial or even benign data can inadvertently compromise their safety. In this paper, we re-examine the fundamental issue of why fine-tuning on non-harmful data still results in safety degradation. We introduce a safety-aware probing (SAP) optimization framework designed to mitigate the safety risks of fine-tuning LLMs. Specifically, SAP incorporates a safety-aware probe into the gradient propagation process, mitigating the model's risk of safety degradation by identifying potential pitfalls in gradient directions, thereby enhancing task-specific performance while successfully preserving model safety. Our extensive experimental results demonstrate that SAP effectively reduces harmfulness below the original fine-tuned model and achieves comparable test loss to standard fine-tuning methods. Our code is available at https://github.com/ChengcanWu/SAP.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Mitigating Fine-tuning Risks in LLMs via Safety-Aware Probing Optimization":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the issue of safety degradation in Large Language Models (LLMs) during fine-tuning, even when using benign datasets. The authors hypothesize that entanglement between useful (task-specific) and safety-critical gradient directions is a key factor. They introduce a Safety-Aware Probing (SAP) optimization framework that incorporates a safety-aware probe into the gradient propagation process. This probe identifies potential safety pitfalls in gradient directions, aiming to enhance task-specific performance while preserving model safety. The experimental results demonstrate that SAP effectively reduces harmfulness compared to standard fine-tuning and several baseline methods, while maintaining comparable test loss. The authors also show SAP's robustness against adversarial attacks and its compatibility with other defense strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel approach to mitigating safety degradation during fine-tuning by using a safety-aware probing mechanism. This is a distinct approach from existing methods that primarily focus on data filtering or parameter regularization. The concept of disentangling useful and safety-critical gradient directions provides a fresh perspective on the problem. The SAP framework's ability to be integrated into various fine-tuning paradigms rather than being restricted to a specific one is also a notable innovation.

**Significance:**  The problem of safety degradation during fine-tuning is significant because it limits the safe deployment of LLMs in real-world applications. Even pre-trained, aligned models can become harmful through seemingly innocuous fine-tuning. The paper offers a practical and effective solution to address this problem, which could contribute to more robust and reliable LLM deployment. The demonstrated robustness against adversarial attacks is also a vital aspect, as it increases the model's resilience against malicious manipulation. The compatibility of SAP with existing defense methods could create even more robust and safer systems, marking a tangible improvement in the overall safety of LLMs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the problem of safety degradation and provides a well-reasoned hypothesis regarding the underlying mechanism.
*   **Novel Approach:** The SAP framework is a unique and innovative approach to the problem, offering a practical and scalable solution.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results consistently demonstrate the effectiveness of SAP across different models, datasets, and attack scenarios.
*   **Scalability:** The framework is not tied to specific fine-tuning paradigms (e.g., LoRA), increasing its applicability.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper includes thorough experiments comparing SAP with various baselines and evaluating its robustness.
*   **Compatibility:**  The paper demonstrates SAP's potential to improve existing defensive strategies, suggesting versatile applicability.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Overhead:**  The computational overhead of SAP (approximately twice the processing time of standard fine-tuning) might be a limiting factor in resource-constrained environments. While the paper notes the GPU memory usage is similar to SFT, the doubled computation time could present a practical barrier for some users.
*   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** Although the paper provides ablation studies, a deeper analysis of the sensitivity of SAP's performance to specific hyperparameter settings would further enhance its practical usability.  While the results are generally consistent, knowing how performance degrades as certain hyperparameters are varied would make SAP easier to adopt.
*   **Theoretical analysis:** The paper can be made stronger if it can provide more theoretical guarantees for the safety-aware probe's effect.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8**.  The paper makes a strong contribution by addressing a critical problem in LLM safety with a novel and empirically validated approach. The findings have important implications for the safe deployment of LLMs. However, the computational overhead, potential hyperparameter sensitivity, and the need for more theoretical analysis prevent it from receiving a higher score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### TRIM: Achieving Extreme Sparsity with Targeted Row-wise Iterative Metric-driven Pruning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16743v1)
- **Authors**: Florentin Beck, William Rudman, Carsten Eickhoff
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) present significant computational and memory challenges due to their extensive size, making pruning essential for their efficient deployment. Existing one-shot pruning methods often apply uniform sparsity constraints across layers or within each layer, resulting in suboptimal performance, especially at high sparsity ratios. This work introduces TRIM (Targeted Row-wise Iterative Metric-driven pruning), a novel approach that applies varying sparsity ratios to individual output dimensions (rows) within each layer. TRIM employs an iterative adjustment process guided by quality metrics to optimize dimension-wise sparsity allocation, focusing on reducing variance in quality retention across outputs to preserve critical information. TRIM can be seamlessly integrated with existing layer-wise pruning strategies. Our evaluations on perplexity and zero-shot tasks across diverse LLM families (Qwen2.5, LLaMA-2, and OPT) and sparsity levels demonstrate that TRIM achieves new state-of-the-art results and enhances stability. For instance, at 80% sparsity, TRIM reduces perplexity by 48% for Qwen2.5-14B and over 90% for OPT-13B compared to baseline methods. We conclude that fine-grained, dimension-wise sparsity adaptation is crucial for pushing the limits of extreme LLM compression. Code available at: https://github.com/flobk/TRIM
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TRIM: Achieving Extreme Sparsity with Targeted Row-wise Iterative Metric-driven Pruning":

**Summary:**

This paper introduces TRIM (Targeted Row-wise Iterative Metric-driven pruning), a novel one-shot pruning method for Large Language Models (LLMs). TRIM addresses the limitations of existing uniform sparsity approaches by applying varying sparsity ratios to individual output dimensions (rows) within each layer.  The algorithm iteratively adjusts these dimension-wise sparsity ratios based on quality metrics, aiming to reduce variance in quality retention across outputs. This allows the model to aggressively prune less critical dimensions while preserving crucial information in others. TRIM is designed to be compatible with existing layer-wise sparsity adaptation techniques and does not require retraining. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art pruning performance at high sparsity levels across multiple LLM families (Qwen2.5, LLaMA-2, and OPT), showing significant perplexity reductions and improvements in zero-shot task performance.  They also provide an analysis explaining why this dimension-wise approach leads to superior results, highlighting the varying sensitivity and importance of different output dimensions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the dimension-wise sparsity adaptation strategy.  Existing pruning methods either enforce uniform sparsity within a layer or adapt sparsity only at the layer level.  TRIM's fine-grained control allows for more effective pruning, particularly at high sparsity ratios.  The iterative adjustment process guided by quality metrics is also a valuable contribution. This makes TRIM stands out from prior works that use Gradient-based methods or Wanda-based per-output comparison.

* **Significance:**  LLM compression is a critical area of research given the computational and memory demands of these models. The paper tackles this challenge by pushing the boundaries of one-shot pruning.  The experimental results clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of TRIM, achieving significant perplexity reductions (e.g., over 90% for OPT-13B at 80% sparsity) and maintaining or improving zero-shot performance. The detailed analysis of why dimension-wise sparsity works and how different dimensions exhibit varying sensitivity adds to the paper's value. In addition, that TRIM can be seamlessly integrated with existing layer-wise pruning strategies expands the application range.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong Empirical Results:**  The experimental results are comprehensive, covering multiple models, sparsity levels, and evaluation metrics.  The improvements over strong baselines (Wanda, OWL, AlphaPruning) are substantial, particularly at high sparsity.
    * **Detailed Analysis:** The paper goes beyond just presenting results by providing insightful analysis into why TRIM works. The analysis of importance score concentration (Gini coefficients), pruning sensitivity, and the impact of output dimensions on performance are valuable contributions.
    * **Compatibility and Integration:** TRIM's compatibility with existing layer-wise methods is a significant advantage, making it easy to incorporate into existing pruning pipelines.
    * **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is clearly written and well-structured, making it easy to understand the proposed method and its benefits.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Iterative nature of the Sparsity Adjustment:** Despite being positioned as a one-shot method, the iterative sparsity adjustment adds computational overhead (though the authors show it's relatively small).  A truly one-shot method without any iteration would be even more desirable.
    * **Dependency on a Quality Metric:** The performance of TRIM depends on the quality metric used to guide the sparsity adjustment. While the authors evaluate several metrics and recommend cosine similarity, the choice of metric could be crucial in different scenarios. It might be beneficial to provide a set of guidelines for choosing the most appropriate metric for a specific use case.
    * **Limited discussion on downstream Applications:** While the paper shows impressive perplexity and zero-shot learning task results, the practical benefits in deployment scenarios (e.g., reduced memory footprint, faster inference) is not directly addressed.

* **Potential Influence:**  TRIM has the potential to influence future research in LLM compression, particularly in the area of one-shot pruning.  The dimension-wise sparsity approach could be adopted and extended by other researchers. The insights into the varying sensitivity and importance of output dimensions could also lead to new pruning techniques and model architectures.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of LLM compression by introducing a novel and effective one-shot pruning method. The strong empirical results and detailed analysis provide convincing evidence of TRIM's benefits.  The compatibility with existing methods and the potential to enable extreme sparsity levels make it a valuable tool for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments.

While the iterative sparsity adjustment and dependency on a quality metric are minor drawbacks, the overall contribution is substantial. It provides a new state-of-the-art results for achieving extreme sparsity. For example, it improves the perplexity of OPT-13B at 80% sparsity from 6461 with Wanda to 324 with TRIM. For all of the reason mentioned above, I assign a score of:

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Self-Rewarding Large Vision-Language Models for Optimizing Prompts in Text-to-Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16763v1)
- **Authors**: Hongji Yang, Yucheng Zhou, Wencheng Han, Jianbing Shen
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image models are powerful for producing high-quality images based on given text prompts, but crafting these prompts often requires specialized vocabulary. To address this, existing methods train rewriting models with supervision from large amounts of manually annotated data and trained aesthetic assessment models. To alleviate the dependence on data scale for model training and the biases introduced by trained models, we propose a novel prompt optimization framework, designed to rephrase a simple user prompt into a sophisticated prompt to a text-to-image model. Specifically, we employ the large vision language models (LVLMs) as the solver to rewrite the user prompt, and concurrently, employ LVLMs as a reward model to score the aesthetics and alignment of the images generated by the optimized prompt. Instead of laborious human feedback, we exploit the prior knowledge of the LVLM to provide rewards, i.e., AI feedback. Simultaneously, the solver and the reward model are unified into one model and iterated in reinforcement learning to achieve self-improvement by giving a solution and judging itself. Results on two popular datasets demonstrate that our method outperforms other strong competitors.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Self-Rewarding Large Vision-Language Models for Optimizing Prompts in Text-to-Image Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel prompt optimization framework for text-to-image generation that leverages Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs).  Instead of relying on large amounts of manually annotated data or trained aesthetic assessment models (common approaches for prompt rewriting), the framework uses an LVLM both as a prompt rewriter (the "solver") and as a reward model to evaluate the aesthetic quality and alignment of the generated images with the original prompt.  This allows for self-improvement through reinforcement learning where the LVLM essentially gives feedback to itself.  The method aims to reduce data dependencies and biases introduced by separately trained reward models. The paper demonstrates that this self-rewarding approach, even with limited training data, can outperform other strong prompt optimization techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of using an LVLM for both prompt rewriting and self-evaluation is a significant contribution. It offers an alternative to data-hungry, human-annotated approaches. The iterative refinement process through self-play is also a valuable concept in this context. The combination of LVLMs, reinforcement learning, and self-supervision is thoughtfully applied to the prompt optimization problem.

*   **Significance:**  The work has the potential to make text-to-image models more accessible to non-experts. By automating prompt engineering, it reduces the need for specialized vocabulary and knowledge. Moreover, by reducing the need for extensive human annotation for training data, it lowers the cost and barriers to entry for developing effective prompt optimization techniques.  The results also suggest that LVLMs can be effectively used for self-supervised learning in complex generation tasks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Reduced Data Dependency:**  The primary strength lies in the significantly reduced reliance on large, high-quality datasets. The approach is more efficient in its data utilization compared to methods that rely on manually annotated prompts or separately trained reward models.
    *   **Dynamic Feedback:** Unlike methods using fixed reward models, the self-rewarding strategy allows the reward model to learn and improve alongside the prompt rewriting model, leading to dynamic feedback throughout training.
    *   **Unified Model:** The approach unifies the solver and reward model, potentially leading to a more coherent and efficient optimization process.
    *   **Strong Results:** The paper demonstrates that the proposed method outperforms several strong baselines on standard datasets.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Resources:** The reliance on large LVLMs is still a significant limitation. While the method reduces data dependencies, it introduces a dependence on substantial computational resources, which could restrict its accessibility.  The paper acknowledges this in the limitations section, but the severity of this issue should be weighed heavily.
    *   **Bias of LVLM:** The LVLM itself could introduce biases into the prompt optimization process. The self-rewarding mechanism relies on the LVLM's internal understanding of aesthetics and alignment, which might not perfectly align with human preferences in all cases. While the human evaluation showed favorability, it may be beneficial to explore the specific types of prompts that the LVLM has bias toward.
    *   **Limited Ablation Study:** While ablation studies are present, more in-depth ablation with different prompt-type (e.g., relevance or aesthetic only) would be helpful to understand each components contribution. Also, a demonstration of the limitations of the approach when dealing with a very different domain (compared to the training dataset).
    *   **Scalability:** The paper mentions a limit to the parameter size. The applicability of this approach with different model parameters is limited and should be expanded to a range of applications.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings are likely to influence the development of more efficient and accessible prompt optimization techniques for text-to-image generation. The self-rewarding approach could be extended to other generative tasks and could inspire further research into self-supervised learning using LVLMs.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7.5**. The paper presents a novel and significant approach to prompt optimization, demonstrating strong performance while reducing data dependency. The primary limitations are the computational resource requirements of LVLMs and the potential for introducing bias based on the LVLM’s understanding of different aspects. Though addressed in a later section, this could introduce significant limitations to the approach.

The results and analysis presented are compelling, but the identified weaknesses and the need for significant computing resources prevent the method from being a truly transformative breakthrough at this stage. I believe that future research addressing these limitations could increase the impact of this approach substantially.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### When Safety Detectors Aren't Enough: A Stealthy and Effective Jailbreak Attack on LLMs via Steganographic Techniques
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16765v1)
- **Authors**: Jianing Geng, Biao Yi, Zekun Fei, Tongxi Wu, Lihai Nie, Zheli Liu
- **Abstract**: Jailbreak attacks pose a serious threat to large language models (LLMs) by bypassing built-in safety mechanisms and leading to harmful outputs. Studying these attacks is crucial for identifying vulnerabilities and improving model security. This paper presents a systematic survey of jailbreak methods from the novel perspective of stealth. We find that existing attacks struggle to simultaneously achieve toxic stealth (concealing toxic content) and linguistic stealth (maintaining linguistic naturalness). Motivated by this, we propose StegoAttack, a fully stealthy jailbreak attack that uses steganography to hide the harmful query within benign, semantically coherent text. The attack then prompts the LLM to extract the hidden query and respond in an encrypted manner. This approach effectively hides malicious intent while preserving naturalness, allowing it to evade both built-in and external safety mechanisms. We evaluate StegoAttack on four safety-aligned LLMs from major providers, benchmarking against eight state-of-the-art methods. StegoAttack achieves an average attack success rate (ASR) of 92.00%, outperforming the strongest baseline by 11.0%. Its ASR drops by less than 1% even under external detection (e.g., Llama Guard). Moreover, it attains the optimal comprehensive scores on stealth detection metrics, demonstrating both high efficacy and exceptional stealth capabilities. The code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/StegoAttack-Jail66
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "When Safety Detectors Aren't Enough: A Stealthy and Effective Jailbreak Attack on LLMs via Steganographic Techniques" introduces StegoAttack, a novel jailbreak method for large language models (LLMs) that leverages steganography to hide malicious queries within benign text. The attack aims to circumvent both built-in and external safety mechanisms by preserving both toxic and linguistic stealth. The process involves embedding the harmful query in the first letters of each sentence in a natural-sounding paragraph. The LLM is then prompted to extract the hidden query and respond in an encrypted manner. The authors evaluate StegoAttack against state-of-the-art methods and demonstrate its superior performance in terms of attack success rate and stealthiness, even when external detectors like Llama Guard are employed. The paper includes a systematic survey of existing jailbreak methods, categorizing them based on their stealth characteristics, and an ablation study to assess the impact of different components of the StegoAttack.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main strength lies in its novel approach to jailbreaking LLMs through steganography. While individual components (jailbreaking, steganography) are not new, their combination in this context, with a focus on simultaneous toxic and linguistic stealth at both input and output level, is a significant advancement. The systematic classification of existing jailbreak attacks based on the concept of stealth adds value to the research. The explicit aim to achieve both toxic *and* linguistic stealth is a clear contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper highlights the limitations of current LLM safety mechanisms and underscores the need for more robust defense strategies. The high success rate of StegoAttack, even against external detectors, demonstrates the vulnerability of state-of-the-art LLMs. This is a significant finding that will likely spur further research into more effective safety mechanisms and adversarial defense techniques. This prompts the field to consider the need for safety systems that can look *beyond* explicit content detection and towards subtle linguistic and semantic signals.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, comparing StegoAttack against eight baselines across four LLMs.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the effectiveness of different components of the StegoAttack.
    *   The authors clearly define the concept of stealth in jailbreak attacks and provide a useful taxonomy.
    *   The feedback dynamic enhancement mechanism shows how to improve the effectiveness of the attack by adapting to model responses.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the paper considers a range of detectors, the evaluation could be strengthened by testing against a wider array, including newer and potentially more robust detection mechanisms.
    *   The paper focuses on single-turn attacks. While this simplifies the analysis, it doesn't reflect the complexity of real-world adversarial scenarios where multi-turn interactions can be used to gradually elicit harmful responses.
    *   The paper mentions different embedding scenarios but does not fully explore the impact of carrier text and scene on attack effectiveness. Some discussion of how these parameters can be further tuned to maximize success is warranted.
    *   The method implicitly relies on the attacker having knowledge of the LLM architecture and/or capabilities since, to work effectively, the malicious request has to be reconstructable with the embedding technique.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence research on both jailbreak attacks and defense mechanisms for LLMs. The StegoAttack approach could inspire new adversarial techniques that focus on concealing malicious intent rather than directly bypassing safety filters. On the defense side, the paper will likely motivate the development of more sophisticated detection methods that are less susceptible to steganographic attacks and can identify subtle signs of malicious intent.

*   **Score Justification:**

    While the individual components aren't entirely novel, the *combination* of steganography, an explicit focus on both toxic *and* linguistic stealth at both input *and* output, and a robust experimental evaluation justifies a strong, but not exceptional, score. The method's effectiveness in circumventing safety mechanisms and its ability to maintain stealth even against external detectors make it a significant contribution. However, the limitations related to single-turn attacks, unexplored embedding scenarios, reliance on reconstructability and the use of relatively few metrics and tests against a limited array of detectors prevent a score in the highest tiers.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### IFEval-Audio: Benchmarking Instruction-Following Capability in Audio-based Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16774v1)
- **Authors**: Yiming Gao, Bin Wang, Chengwei Wei, Shuo Sun, AiTi Aw
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong instruction-following capabilities in text-based tasks. However, this ability often deteriorates in multimodal models after alignment with non-text modalities such as images or audio. While several recent efforts have investigated instruction-following performance in text and vision-language models, instruction-following in audio-based large language models remains largely unexplored. To bridge this gap, we introduce IFEval-Audio, a novel evaluation dataset designed to assess the ability to follow instructions in an audio LLM. IFEval-Audio contains 280 audio-instruction-answer triples across six diverse dimensions: Content, Capitalization, Symbol, List Structure, Length, and Format. Each example pairs an audio input with a text instruction, requiring the model to generate an output that follows a specified structure. We benchmark state-of-the-art audio LLMs on their ability to follow audio-involved instructions. The dataset is released publicly to support future research in this emerging area.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "IFEval-Audio: Benchmarking Instruction-Following Capability in Audio-based Large Language Models."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces IFEval-Audio, a new dataset designed to evaluate the instruction-following capabilities of audio-based Large Language Models (LLMs).  The dataset comprises 280 audio-instruction-answer triples covering six dimensions: Content, Capitalization, Symbol, List Structure, Length, and Format.  It includes audio from various sources (speech, music, and environmental sounds) and provides both rule-based and LLM-based evaluation methods.  The authors benchmarked several state-of-the-art audio LLMs using IFEval-Audio and identified performance variations and weaknesses across the different dimensions. The dataset and code are publicly released.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a clear gap in the evaluation of multimodal LLMs. While instruction-following has been extensively studied in text and image, audio has been largely overlooked.  IFEval-Audio is a novel dataset specifically designed for this purpose.  The inclusion of diverse audio sources and the consideration of semantic correctness in addition to format adherence contribute to the novelty. However, other multimodal datasets exist and include audio, even if in a more limited fashion.

*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the development of audio-based LLMs.  By providing a benchmark and evaluation framework, it can guide future research in this area and encourage the development of more robust and instruction-following models.  The findings from the benchmarked models highlight specific areas where improvement is needed. The public release of the dataset will further facilitate research.  A limitation is that this dataset is relatively small, limiting the generalizability of these results to more complex audio instruction tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition:  The paper clearly identifies the gap in audio instruction-following evaluation.
    *   Well-designed dataset: The IFEval-Audio dataset appears to be carefully constructed with diverse audio sources and instruction types.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation: The combination of rule-based and LLM-based evaluation methods provides a thorough assessment.
    *   Public release:  The dataset and code are publicly available, promoting further research.
    *   Highlights specific performance gaps: The benchmarking reveals specific challenges for current models in areas such as capitalization and complex formatting.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited Scale: The dataset is relatively small (280 examples). Larger datasets are needed for a robust assessment.
    *   English-only: The audio and instructions are only in English, limiting its applicability to multilingual scenarios.
    *   Dependence on judge LLM: The LLM-based semantic evaluation introduces dependence on the judge model's biases and limitations. The semantic correctness score will be heavily influenced by the LLM judge.
    *   Limited Scope: It is specifically designed for format adherence and content correctness, neglecting other relevant aspects like reasoning or creativity.

**Justification:**

The paper provides a valuable contribution by addressing a gap in multimodal LLM evaluation. It introduces a novel and well-designed dataset specifically for audio instruction-following, which is a relatively unexplored area. The evaluation framework and benchmarked results offer valuable insights for future research. However, the dataset's limited scale, English-only focus, and dependence on a judge LLM for semantic evaluation are significant limitations. Given these considerations, the paper is a good start for the field but requires considerable improvement in the scale and multilingual support in future iterations.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Reasoning Beyond Language: A Comprehensive Survey on Latent Chain-of-Thought Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16782v1)
- **Authors**: Xinghao Chen, Anhao Zhao, Heming Xia, Xuan Lu, Hanlin Wang, Yanjun Chen, Wei Zhang, Jian Wang, Wenjie Li, Xiaoyu Shen
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved impressive performance on complex reasoning tasks with Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting. However, conventional CoT relies on reasoning steps explicitly verbalized in natural language, introducing inefficiencies and limiting its applicability to abstract reasoning. To address this, there has been growing research interest in latent CoT reasoning, where inference occurs within latent spaces. By decoupling reasoning from language, latent reasoning promises richer cognitive representations and more flexible, faster inference. Researchers have explored various directions in this promising field, including training methodologies, structural innovations, and internal reasoning mechanisms. This paper presents a comprehensive overview and analysis of this reasoning paradigm. We begin by proposing a unified taxonomy from four perspectives: token-wise strategies, internal mechanisms, analysis, and applications. We then provide in-depth discussions and comparative analyses of representative methods, highlighting their design patterns, strengths, and open challenges. We aim to provide a structured foundation for advancing this emerging direction in LLM reasoning. The relevant papers will be regularly updated at https://github.com/EIT-NLP/Awesome-Latent-CoT.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper, including a novelty and significance score.

**Summary:**

This paper is a survey on latent chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning in large language models (LLMs). It addresses the limitations of traditional, explicit CoT, which relies on verbalized reasoning steps, introducing inefficiencies and hindering abstract reasoning. The survey proposes a unified taxonomy for latent CoT approaches, categorizing methods based on token-wise strategies, internal mechanisms, analysis, and applications. It then provides in-depth discussions and comparative analyses of representative methods, highlighting their design patterns, strengths, and open challenges. The authors aim to provide a structured foundation for advancing this emerging direction in LLM reasoning. The paper identifies critical open problems and promising directions for future research. The paper concludes by examining the applications of this type of reasoning across various domains.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in its comprehensive and structured overview of a rapidly developing field. While individual techniques within latent CoT might have been described elsewhere, the effort to create a unified taxonomy and a detailed comparative analysis represents a significant contribution. The paper identifies the main research threads and the important trade-offs in various approaches which is beneficial to researchers wanting to quickly understand the field. The survey also highlights the challenges facing this field in terms of difficulty of training, generalization issues, and interpretability, and points towards potential solutions.
*   **Significance:** Latent CoT is a very important topic as explicit CoT presents challenges to efficiency and applicability to abstract concepts. As LLMs become more integrated into real-world applications, the ability to reason efficiently and abstractly is crucial. This survey offers a timely and valuable resource for researchers and practitioners interested in this area. The survey highlights the limitations of current approaches and provides directions for future research, which will hopefully accelerate research and innovation in this area.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey covers a wide range of techniques and perspectives within the latent CoT field.
    *   **Structured Taxonomy:** The proposed taxonomy provides a clear and organized framework for understanding different approaches.
    *   **In-depth Analysis:** The comparative analyses of representative methods are insightful and highlight key strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **Timeliness:** The survey addresses a rapidly evolving field and offers a current snapshot of the research landscape.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Potential for Rapid Obsolescence:** Given the speed of development in the field, some of the specific techniques discussed may become outdated relatively quickly. This is an unavoidable limitation of any survey in a fast-moving area.
    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** While the survey discusses various techniques, it does not offer extensive empirical comparisons or meta-analyses of their performance. This is understandable, as the focus is on providing an overview rather than a benchmark.
    *   **Subjectivity:** Some of the categorizations and interpretations might be subjective, reflecting the authors' perspectives on the field.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper provides a well-organized and insightful overview of a rapidly evolving field. Its main strengths are its comprehensive coverage, clear taxonomy, and insightful analysis of representative methods. The paper's contribution lies primarily in organizing existing work and highlighting open challenges, and is a valuable resource for researchers who want to understand latent CoT. While surveys naturally have limitations due to the speed of development in the field and potential subjectivity, the benefits provided are significant and outweigh the weaknesses.
