### CoTSRF: Utilize Chain of Thought as Stealthy and Robust Fingerprint of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16785v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenzhen Ren, GuoBiao Li, Sheng Li, Zhenxing Qian, Xinpeng Zhang
- **Abstract**: Despite providing superior performance, open-source large language models (LLMs) are vulnerable to abusive usage. To address this issue, recent works propose LLM fingerprinting methods to identify the specific source LLMs behind suspect applications. However, these methods fail to provide stealthy and robust fingerprint verification. In this paper, we propose a novel LLM fingerprinting scheme, namely CoTSRF, which utilizes the Chain of Thought (CoT) as the fingerprint of an LLM. CoTSRF first collects the responses from the source LLM by querying it with crafted CoT queries. Then, it applies contrastive learning to train a CoT extractor that extracts the CoT feature (i.e., fingerprint) from the responses. Finally, CoTSRF conducts fingerprint verification by comparing the Kullback-Leibler divergence between the CoT features of the source and suspect LLMs against an empirical threshold. Various experiments have been conducted to demonstrate the advantage of our proposed CoTSRF for fingerprinting LLMs, particularly in stealthy and robust fingerprint verification.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper "CoTSRF: Utilize Chain of Thought as Stealthy and Robust Fingerprint of Large Language Models" proposes a novel method for fingerprinting LLMs to detect abusive usage and license infringement. The core idea is to leverage the Chain of Thought (CoT) process, which is argued to be a unique characteristic of an LLM's reasoning pattern, as the fingerprint. The method, named CoTSRF, involves crafting CoT queries, collecting responses from the source LLM and suspect LLMs, training a CoT extractor using contrastive learning, and comparing the CoT features using KL divergence. A High-Temperature Data Augmentation (HTDA) strategy is used to create diverse responses for training the CoT extractor. The paper claims that CoTSRF offers improved stealthiness and robustness compared to existing methods. Experiments demonstrate that CoTSRF achieves competitive results in identifying infringing LLMs while being less susceptible to prompt filtering and output perturbations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents an interesting and well-motivated approach to LLM fingerprinting. The idea of utilizing CoT as a fingerprint is novel and leverages a characteristic that is increasingly considered intrinsic to LLMs. The motivation for a stealthy and robust fingerprinting method is clearly articulated, given the potential for malicious actors to circumvent less sophisticated methods.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty of the approach:** Using CoT as a fingerprint is a relatively unexplored area. While the concept of CoT is not new, its application to fingerprinting adds a fresh perspective.
*   **Emphasis on stealthiness and robustness:** The paper addresses key weaknesses of prior work (TRAP) by focusing on stealthiness (resistance to prompt filtering) and robustness (resistance to output perturbation and fine-tuning).
*   **Sound methodology:** The proposed CoTSRF framework includes several well-designed components, such as the HTDA strategy and the contrastive learning setup for training the CoT extractor.
*   **Comprehensive experiments:** The experiments cover various aspects of the method's performance, including effectiveness, reliability, stealthiness, and robustness against different attacks. The comparison with TRAP provides a clear benchmark.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalizability Argument:** The success hinges on whether CoT features are really model-specific, but the evaluation is limited to only 3 models when used to extract a fingerprint, though more are used when evaluating reliability. The generalizability needs further, broader testing, in particular with the Mixture of Experts models mentioned as future work.
*   **Definition of Stealthiness:** While the paper uses perplexity to justify steathiness, and the results are improved, there is no evidence of how good that is for real world use. Has that perplexity threshold been reached by a malicious user looking to circumvent the CoTSRF fingerprinting process?
*   **Reliance on empirical threshold:** The final fingerprint verification relies on comparing the KL divergence against an empirical threshold. The process for determining this threshold and its sensitivity to different datasets or models is not thoroughly discussed. The paper does not account for the sensitivity of the threshold and how sensitive it might be in a changing environment. The claim is made that the method is robust to fine-tuning, but that fine-tuning occurred on the 'timdettmers/openassistant-guanaco' dataset. What happens if the user decides to fine-tune on something more general purpose, such as webtext?

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to make a significant contribution to the field of LLM security and responsible AI. As LLMs become more pervasive, the need for methods to protect against abusive usage and license infringement will only increase. CoTSRF offers a promising approach that addresses some of the key challenges in this area. The main contribution is the initial framework, but there's a limited amount of rigor to it.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution by introducing a novel and well-engineered method for LLM fingerprinting. The emphasis on stealthiness and robustness is particularly relevant. However, the limited experimental setup, the reliance on an empirical threshold, and the somewhat weak argument for why the fingerprint can actually be used to discriminate LLMs from one another detract from the paper's overall impact. Although the CoT idea is novel, the paper needs more rigor, especially when considering other models and larger data sets for the fingerprints. Given the current set of claims versus evidence presented, the paper merits a score between 6-7.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Accidental Misalignment: Fine-Tuning Language Models Induces Unexpected Vulnerability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16789v1)
- **Authors**: Punya Syon Pandey, Samuel Simko, Kellin Pelrine, Zhijing Jin
- **Abstract**: As large language models gain popularity, their vulnerability to adversarial attacks remains a primary concern. While fine-tuning models on domain-specific datasets is often employed to improve model performance, it can introduce vulnerabilities within the underlying model. In this work, we investigate Accidental Misalignment, unexpected vulnerabilities arising from characteristics of fine-tuning data. We begin by identifying potential correlation factors such as linguistic features, semantic similarity, and toxicity within our experimental datasets. We then evaluate the adversarial performance of these fine-tuned models and assess how dataset factors correlate with attack success rates. Lastly, we explore potential causal links, offering new insights into adversarial defense strategies and highlighting the crucial role of dataset design in preserving model alignment. Our code is available at https://github.com/psyonp/accidental_misalignment.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided paper. Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates "Accidental Misalignment" in large language models (LLMs), where fine-tuning on seemingly benign or domain-specific datasets inadvertently increases the model's vulnerability to adversarial attacks and jailbreaking. The authors examine the impact of various dataset characteristics (linguistic features, semantic similarity, toxicity, readability) on this vulnerability. They fine-tune LLaMA 3.1 8B on a variety of datasets (benign, engineering, cybersecurity, legal, and harmful), analyze the correlation between dataset features and attack success rates using methods like GCG, AutoPrompt, and PEZ (from the HarmBench framework), and perform feature intervention experiments by ablating certain features from datasets. The results indicate that certain dataset factors, like longer response lengths, prompt toxicity, and prompt Type-Token Ratio, are positively correlated with increased adversarial vulnerability, while others, like Type-Token Ratio of responses and prompt sentiment, show a negative correlation. Feature intervention experiments further support the link between these factors and model alignment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Interesting and Relevant Problem:** The paper addresses a practically important problem of LLM safety and robustness in the context of fine-tuning. The idea of "accidental misalignment" is compelling and highlights a subtle but significant pitfall in how LLMs are often trained.
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper uses a reasonably comprehensive empirical approach. It considers a good range of datasets, adversarial attack techniques, and dataset characteristics.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The methodology includes appropriate statistical analysis (Spearman correlations) and feature intervention experiments to explore potential causal links. The use of HarmBench offers a standardized evaluation environment.
    *   **Actionable Insights:** The findings provide actionable insights for designing safer fine-tuning datasets. Identifying specific characteristics that correlate with increased vulnerability allows for more informed dataset curation and pre-screening.
    *   **Open-Source Code:** The availability of the code contributes to the reproducibility and transparency of the research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Models:** The study is primarily based on LLaMA 3.1 8B Instruct, a single model architecture and the generalizability to other models architectures (e.g., models with inherently different safegurads or those that explicitly use reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) for alignment) is limited. While the cross-model analysis provides some insights, more extensive experiments with different models would significantly strengthen the findings.
    *   **Dataset Size and Representativeness:** While the datasets are varied, some might be considered relatively small (e.g., Legal with only 500 samples). The representativeness of these datasets for their respective domains is also a potential concern. Also, the details of how these datasets were crafted could inform potential areas for intervention.
    *   **Correlation vs. Causation:** While the intervention experiments offer stronger evidence, the primary analysis relies on correlations. The relationships between dataset features and adversarial vulnerability may be more complex than simple linear correlations, and there could be confounding factors not accounted for.
    *   **Univariate Ablations:** The intervention analysis uses univariate ablations, which simplifies the potential interactions between multiple factors. More sophisticated ablation methods (e.g., considering feature interactions) may provide better insight.
    *   **Limited discussion of feature engineering techniques** The authors do not provide much detail in how specific features were identified or engineered. As such, the results from the Spearman rank correlation analysis are difficult to interpret.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper does make a valuable contribution by highlighting and empirically demonstrating the phenomenon of "accidental misalignment" during fine-tuning.
    *   It moves beyond simply observing that fine-tuning can break safety to identifying specific dataset features that contribute to this vulnerability.
    *   The actionable insights on dataset design are practically useful for practitioners working with LLMs.
    *   The work, while not radically new, offers a novel perspective and provides a useful framework for further research.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper is a worthwhile contribution to the field of LLM safety and alignment. The empirical analysis is well-conducted, the insights are actionable, and the problem is of clear practical importance. However, the limitations related to model scope, dataset size, and the correlation vs. causation issue prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking work. The paper offers a new angle on understanding failure modes in fine-tuned language models and paves the way for more robust defense strategies. It emphasizes the crucial, yet often overlooked, role of dataset design in preserving model alignment.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Learning Flexible Forward Trajectories for Masked Molecular Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16790v1)
- **Authors**: Hyunjin Seo, Taewon Kim, Sihyun Yu, SungSoo Ahn
- **Abstract**: Masked diffusion models (MDMs) have achieved notable progress in modeling discrete data, while their potential in molecular generation remains underexplored. In this work, we explore their potential and introduce the surprising result that naively applying standards MDMs severely degrades the performance. We identify the critical cause of this issue as a state-clashing problem-where the forward diffusion of distinct molecules collapse into a common state, resulting in a mixture of reconstruction targets that cannot be learned using typical reverse diffusion process with unimodal predictions. To mitigate this, we propose Masked Element-wise Learnable Diffusion (MELD) that orchestrates per-element corruption trajectories to avoid collision between distinct molecular graphs. This is achieved through a parameterized noise scheduling network that assigns distinct corruption rates to individual graph elements, i.e., atoms and bonds. Extensive experiments on diverse molecular benchmarks reveal that MELD markedly enhances overall generation quality compared to element-agnostic noise scheduling, increasing the chemical validity of vanilla MDMs on ZINC250K from 15% to 93%, Furthermore, it achieves state-of-the-art property alignment in conditional generation tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the under-explored potential of Masked Diffusion Models (MDMs) in molecular generation. It reveals that directly applying standard MDMs degrades performance due to a "state-clashing" problem where distinct molecules converge to similar intermediate states during the forward diffusion process. The authors propose Masked Element-wise Learnable Diffusion (MELD) to mitigate this issue. MELD uses a parameterized noise scheduling network that assigns distinct corruption rates to individual graph elements (atoms and bonds) to prevent collision between molecular graphs. Experiments on diverse molecular benchmarks demonstrate that MELD significantly enhances generation quality and achieves state-of-the-art property alignment in conditional generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in identifying the "state-clashing" problem specific to applying MDMs to molecular graphs.  Existing literature largely focuses on adapting MDMs from image and text domains without explicitly addressing the unique challenges posed by molecular structures and chemical rules. The proposed MELD framework, which learns per-element corruption trajectories, is a reasonably novel solution to this problem. The key idea of avoiding premature collapse to indistinguishable intermediate states is a crucial contribution.

*   **Significance:**  The paper holds significant potential for advancing molecular generation. Overcoming limitations that prevent MDMs from achieving satisfactory validity or property alignment is critical for making these models practical for real-world applications like drug discovery and material design. The results demonstrate substantial improvements in chemical validity, a major roadblock in previous MDM applications for molecules. The state-of-the-art property alignment performance in conditional generation is also noteworthy. The discovery that the denoiser module prefers to unmask graph elements in a node-first manner, and the variance observed in the learned noise schedule in the later phases of the noising process are important insights. These discoveries highlight the importance of avoiding state-clashing.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem identification (state-clashing).
    *   Well-motivated and designed solution (MELD with learnable noise scheduling).
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across diverse datasets and metrics.
    *   Ablation studies that highlight the effectiveness of element-wise scheduling over class-wise or fixed schedules.
    *   Qualitative analysis providing insights into MELD's denoising process.
    *   Significant gains in chemical validity compared to baseline MDMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Although substantial, validity is not consistently at 100% across all the evaluated datasets, which is ideally desired for generating molecules intended for downstream tasks.
    *   The paper could benefit from further exploration of the learned noise schedules. Understanding the specific corruption rates assigned to different atom types and bond types would provide more chemical intuition and potentially lead to even better designs.
    *   The limitation regarding the inherent multimodality, particularly when many molecules are masked, needs to be addressed for comprehensive performance.
    *   While comparisons are made against a strong GraphDiT baseline, the results against graphdiit do not show overwhelming improvement in property alignment.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in molecular generation by:
    *   Highlighting the importance of considering molecular-specific challenges when adapting general generative models.
    *   Encouraging the development of more sophisticated forward diffusion processes that prevent state-clashing.
    *   Providing a strong baseline (MELD) for future MDM-based molecular generation methods.
    *   Offering insights that can be potentially transferred into other discrete structure modeling domains.

**Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by identifying and addressing a key limitation of MDMs in the context of molecular generation. The proposed MELD framework is well-designed, experimentally validated, and demonstrates significant improvements. It bridges an important gap in the literature by offering a targeted solution to the state-clashing problem. However, the observed performance enhancements and lack of consistent 100% validity, there's still scope for improvement, and more in-depth insights into the learned noise schedules would strengthen the work.

### REPA Works Until It Doesn't: Early-Stopped, Holistic Alignment Supercharges Diffusion Training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16792v1)
- **Authors**: Ziqiao Wang, Wangbo Zhao, Yuhao Zhou, Zekai Li, Zhiyuan Liang, Mingjia Shi, Xuanlei Zhao, Pengfei Zhou, Kaipeng Zhang, Zhangyang Wang, Kai Wang, Yang You
- **Abstract**: Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) deliver state-of-the-art image quality, yet their training remains notoriously slow. A recent remedy -- representation alignment (REPA) that matches DiT hidden features to those of a non-generative teacher (e.g. DINO) -- dramatically accelerates the early epochs but plateaus or even degrades performance later. We trace this failure to a capacity mismatch: once the generative student begins modelling the joint data distribution, the teacher's lower-dimensional embeddings and attention patterns become a straitjacket rather than a guide. We then introduce HASTE (Holistic Alignment with Stage-wise Termination for Efficient training), a two-phase schedule that keeps the help and drops the hindrance. Phase I applies a holistic alignment loss that simultaneously distills attention maps (relational priors) and feature projections (semantic anchors) from the teacher into mid-level layers of the DiT, yielding rapid convergence. Phase II then performs one-shot termination that deactivates the alignment loss, once a simple trigger such as a fixed iteration is hit, freeing the DiT to focus on denoising and exploit its generative capacity. HASTE speeds up training of diverse DiTs without architecture changes. On ImageNet 256X256, it reaches the vanilla SiT-XL/2 baseline FID in 50 epochs and matches REPA's best FID in 500 epochs, amounting to a 28X reduction in optimization steps. HASTE also improves text-to-image DiTs on MS-COCO, demonstrating to be a simple yet principled recipe for efficient diffusion training across various tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/NUS-HPC-AI-Lab/HASTE .
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "REPA Works Until It Doesn't: Early-Stopped, Holistic Alignment Supercharges Diffusion Training":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of slow training in Diffusion Transformers (DiTs), particularly when using Representation Alignment (REPA). REPA accelerates initial training epochs by aligning DiT features with a non-generative teacher (e.g., DINO), but its performance plateaus or degrades later in training. The authors attribute this to a capacity mismatch: the teacher's lower-dimensional representation becomes a bottleneck as the DiT learns the full joint data distribution.  They introduce HASTE (Holistic Alignment with Stage-wise Termination for Efficient training), a two-phase approach. Phase I uses a holistic alignment loss that simultaneously distills attention maps and feature projections from the teacher. Phase II performs one-shot termination, deactivating the alignment loss after a trigger (e.g., a fixed iteration), allowing the DiT to focus on denoising. HASTE speeds up DiT training on ImageNet and MS-COCO without architectural changes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in diagnosing *why* REPA's benefits diminish over time and proposing a principled solution (HASTE) based on that diagnosis. While representation alignment itself isn't new, the gradient-angle analysis revealing the conflict between alignment and denoising objectives is a significant contribution. The idea of stage-wise termination is intuitive, but the justification provided, supported by both qualitative and quantitative evidence, strengthens its significance. The holistic alignment (attention + features) also makes sense, as it captures both relational and semantic priors from the teacher.

*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from HASTE's practical impact on diffusion model training. Achieving a 28x speedup to match vanilla SiT-XL/2 performance is considerable. More generally, diffusion models are computationally expensive, and efficient training methods like HASTE are valuable.  The approach is also promising because it's architecture-agnostic and can be applied to various DiTs. Furthermore, it provides a better understanding of using external guidance during diffusion training, suggesting that teacher signals are not always uniformly helpful.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies a limitation of existing methods (REPA).
    *   **Strong Justification:**  The capacity mismatch hypothesis is well-motivated and supported by gradient-angle analysis and the low-frequency experiment.
    *   **Principled Solution:** HASTE is a logical solution given the problem analysis.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments cover multiple DiT architectures and datasets. The ablation studies carefully examine the effects of different components.
    *   **Practical Impact:**  The speedup and generalizability of HASTE are significant.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **One-Shot Termination Simplicity:** The termination strategy, while effective, relies on a rather simple fixed iteration trigger (though the paper mentions a gradient-angle trigger as an alternative). A more adaptive termination criterion, perhaps based on real-time monitoring of the gradient similarity, could be more robust.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Attention Alignment Depth:**  While the paper mentions the importance of the right depth for attention alignment, the experiments don't exhaustively explore all possible layer combinations.
    *   **Limited Application to Other Model Classes**: While it will require more testing, one would expect this technique to generalize well to any similar distillation of teacher-student models where capacity mismatch is a concern.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research in diffusion model training by encouraging a more nuanced understanding of representation alignment. It highlights the importance of adapting training strategies based on the evolving capacity of the generative model. It may also inspire the development of more sophisticated termination criteria for alignment-based methods.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper provides a novel and insightful diagnosis of a limitation in existing representation alignment techniques. The proposed solution, HASTE, is effective and leads to significant performance improvements. The experiments are thorough, and the paper is well-written.

However, the termination strategy, while effective, seems a bit simplistic and could benefit from more adaptive approaches. Also, though the speedups are solid, they are improvements on an already slow baseline. The novelty is a well-justified method that works well, but it is not a new *paradigm* for training, and some components still feel slightly "engineered" for the DiT setup, which lowers the overall score. HASTE does demonstrate a good contribution that should serve as an important reference for researchers.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### SEED: Speaker Embedding Enhancement Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16798v1)
- **Authors**: KiHyun Nam, Jungwoo Heo, Jee-weon Jung, Gangin Park, Chaeyoung Jung, Ha-Jin Yu, Joon Son Chung
- **Abstract**: A primary challenge when deploying speaker recognition systems in real-world applications is performance degradation caused by environmental mismatch. We propose a diffusion-based method that takes speaker embeddings extracted from a pre-trained speaker recognition model and generates refined embeddings. For training, our approach progressively adds Gaussian noise to both clean and noisy speaker embeddings extracted from clean and noisy speech, respectively, via forward process of a diffusion model, and then reconstructs them to clean embeddings in the reverse process. While inferencing, all embeddings are regenerated via diffusion process. Our method needs neither speaker label nor any modification to the existing speaker recognition pipeline. Experiments on evaluation sets simulating environment mismatch scenarios show that our method can improve recognition accuracy by up to 19.6% over baseline models while retaining performance on conventional scenarios. We publish our code here https://github.com/kaistmm/seed-pytorch
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SEED: Speaker Embedding Enhancement Diffusion Model" proposes a novel method for improving speaker recognition performance in noisy environments.  The approach uses a Diffusion Probabilistic Model (DPM) to refine speaker embeddings extracted from both clean and noisy speech. The core idea is to progressively add Gaussian noise to both types of embeddings (clean and noisy) and then train a reverse process to reconstruct the clean embeddings. This process aims to minimize the discrepancy between embeddings of the same speaker extracted under different environmental conditions.  The method is label-free, can be applied on top of existing speaker recognition pipelines, and exhibits improved accuracy in mismatched environments compared to baseline models and a DRL based method.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The application of diffusion models at the embedding level for speaker recognition is a novel contribution.  While diffusion models have been used for speech enhancement (waveform or spectrogram level), applying them directly to speaker embeddings is a less explored area. This is a strength of the paper.
    *   The training strategy, using pairs of clean and augmented noisy speech to generate embeddings for the diffusion model, allows for effective learning of a mapping between noisy and clean embedding spaces. This use of augmentations is a relatively standard technique but it is combined effectively with the diffusion model.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper demonstrates significant improvements in speaker recognition accuracy, particularly in mismatched conditions, which is a critical challenge in real-world deployments. The results suggest that SEED is effective at removing or mitigating the effects of environmental noise on speaker embeddings.
    *   The simplicity and ease of integration of SEED are valuable.  It does not require retraining the underlying speaker recognition model or significant architectural modifications, making it a practical and versatile approach. Also it does not require the speaker labels.
    *   The comparison with an existing audio enhancement method provides useful insight into the benefits of embedding-level manipulation over waveform-level processing.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem definition and motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of domain mismatch in speaker recognition.
    *   **Novel application of DPMs:**  Applying diffusion at the embedding level is a creative approach.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The paper evaluates SEED across several datasets, including both generalisation and environment robustness datasets.
    *   **Quantitative results:** The reported improvements in EER and minDCF are substantial, especially in noisy environments.
    *   **Practical benefits:**  The method is shown to be efficient and easy to integrate into existing systems.
    *   **Comparison with existing techniques:** A thorough comparison to existing state-of-the-art techniques is included.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper provides limited analysis of the learned embeddings. It would be insightful to visualize the embedding space and understand how SEED alters the distribution of embeddings from different speakers and conditions.  The paper does not provide an ablation study analysing the relative contributions of each component.
    *   The method is based on the assumption that noisy and clean embeddings from the same speaker are relatively close in the embedding space. While this is a reasonable assumption, the authors could provide more justification or analysis to support this claim.
    *   While efficient, the training is conducted on a single GPU. The resource usage and training complexity are not extensively investigated.

*   **Impact and Influence:**

    *   The paper has the potential to influence the development of more robust speaker recognition systems, particularly for deployment in noisy or variable environments.
    *   The approach of applying diffusion models at the embedding level could be extended to other representation learning tasks in speech and audio processing.
    *   The method may be adapted for domains where labeled data is scarce, since it does not require speaker labels during training.

*Justification of Score:*

The paper presents a novel application of diffusion models for speaker embedding enhancement, achieving substantial improvements in speaker recognition accuracy under mismatched conditions. The method is easy to integrate, does not require additional labels, and is computationally efficient. While there is room for further analysis of the learned embeddings and the impact of various architectural choices, the paper makes a solid contribution to the field.

### Learning Beyond Limits: Multitask Learning and Synthetic Data for Low-Resource Canonical Morpheme Segmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16800v1)
- **Authors**: Changbing Yang, Garrett Nicolai
- **Abstract**: We introduce a transformer-based morpheme segmentation system that augments a low-resource training signal through multitask learning and LLM-generated synthetic data. Our framework jointly predicts morphological segments and glosses from orthographic input, leveraging shared linguistic representations obtained through a common documentary process to enhance model generalization. To further address data scarcity, we integrate synthetic training data generated by large language models (LLMs) using in-context learning. Experimental results on the SIGMORPHON 2023 dataset show that our approach significantly improves word-level segmentation accuracy and morpheme-level F1-score across multiple low-resource languages.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a transformer-based system for canonical morpheme segmentation, especially targeting low-resource languages. The core idea is to augment the training data using two key strategies: multitask learning and LLM-generated synthetic data. The multitask approach trains the model to jointly predict morphological segments and glosses from orthographic input. Synthetic data is created using in-context learning with large language models (LLMs) to address data scarcity. Experiments on the SIGMORPHON 2023 dataset demonstrate improvements in word-level segmentation accuracy and morpheme-level F1 score across multiple low-resource languages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a critical problem:** Morpheme segmentation is fundamental to linguistic analysis, and the paper tackles the challenges faced in low-resource scenarios where annotated data is scarce.
*   **Well-motivated approach:** The paper clearly articulates the rationale behind using both multitask learning and synthetic data. Multitask learning leverages existing glossing information without extra annotation effort, while synthetic data combats label bias and improves generalization.
*   **Strong experimental results:** The experiments demonstrate significant improvements over baseline models and the TAMS system, showing the effectiveness of the proposed techniques. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of each component.
*   **Qualitative analysis:** The paper includes a qualitative analysis that suggests that multitask learning reduces unnecessary modifications during segmentation, leading to more accurate results.
*   **Ethical considerations:** The paper acknowledges the potential risks associated with the misuse of LLM-generated synthetic data and emphasizes the importance of respecting the communities whose languages are being studied.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM dependency:** The reliance on LLM-generated data introduces uncertainties regarding linguistic validity and potential hallucinations. While the paper acknowledges this limitation, a more detailed qualitative error analysis would be beneficial.
*   **Limited novelty in methodology:** The individual components (multitask learning, LLM-generated data) are not entirely novel in themselves. The main contribution lies in the combination of these techniques for this specific task and domain. The methodology used is largely a variation and combination of other works.
*   **Vocabulary limitations:** The LLM-generated data is limited by the vocabulary present in the training set. This restricts its ability to model truly novel linguistic forms.
*   **Inconsistent improvements:** While the approach shows overall improvements, some languages benefit more than others. The reasons for these variations are not fully explored. Some languages like Lezgi showed an improvement until certain levels of extra data and then degraded in performance. High performing languages like Tsez and Nyangbo are difficult to improve further. This needs a deeper investigation.
*   **Lack of novelty:** There is no "game-changing" innovation or substantial theoretical contribution. The novelty is more about a specific application and combination of existing techniques rather than a disruptive breakthrough.
*   **No head to head Comparison:** Authors compare against results from previously published papers which might suffer from different experimental setups.

**Significance:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of low-resource language processing by demonstrating the effectiveness of combining multitask learning and LLM-generated data for morpheme segmentation. The approach has the potential to improve the development of computational tools for linguistic documentation and preservation. However, the limitations related to LLM dependency and vocabulary coverage should be addressed in future research.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-executed study that addresses a relevant problem. The results are promising and demonstrate the potential of the proposed techniques. However, the limited novelty in methodology and the reliance on LLMs prevent it from achieving a higher score. More work is needed to address the limitations and validate the linguistic validity of the synthetic data. In addition, there is no significant theoretical advancement in this work. It is more of an engineering effort to combine existing solutions.

### SOLVE: Synergy of Language-Vision and End-to-End Networks for Autonomous Driving
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16805v1)
- **Authors**: Xuesong Chen, Linjiang Huang, Tao Ma, Rongyao Fang, Shaoshuai Shi, Hongsheng Li
- **Abstract**: The integration of Vision-Language Models (VLMs) into autonomous driving systems has shown promise in addressing key challenges such as learning complexity, interpretability, and common-sense reasoning. However, existing approaches often struggle with efficient integration and realtime decision-making due to computational demands. In this paper, we introduce SOLVE, an innovative framework that synergizes VLMs with end-to-end (E2E) models to enhance autonomous vehicle planning. Our approach emphasizes knowledge sharing at the feature level through a shared visual encoder, enabling comprehensive interaction between VLM and E2E components. We propose a Trajectory Chain-of-Thought (T-CoT) paradigm, which progressively refines trajectory predictions, reducing uncertainty and improving accuracy. By employing a temporal decoupling strategy, SOLVE achieves efficient cooperation by aligning high-quality VLM outputs with E2E real-time performance. Evaluated on the nuScenes dataset, our method demonstrates significant improvements in trajectory prediction accuracy, paving the way for more robust and reliable autonomous driving systems.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, focusing on its novelty and significance, and concluding with a score.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SOLVE, a novel framework for autonomous driving that integrates Vision-Language Models (VLMs) with end-to-end (E2E) driving models.  The key idea is to achieve synergistic cooperation between VLMs and E2E models at both the feature level (through a shared visual encoder and a Sequential Q-Former) and the trajectory level.  A Trajectory Chain-of-Thought (T-CoT) paradigm is proposed, where trajectories are progressively refined using a predefined trajectory bank and chain-style reasoning. A temporal decoupling strategy allows the E2E model to asynchronously utilize VLM-generated trajectories as initialization priors to improve real-time performance.  The paper claims significant improvements in trajectory prediction accuracy on the nuScenes dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the synergistic integration of VLMs and E2E models at *both* the feature and trajectory levels, with a strong focus on efficiency. While prior works have explored VLM integration, they often rely on post-processing VLM outputs or have limited feature-level interaction. The Sequential Q-Former (SQ-Former) is a relatively novel component aimed at efficiently compressing visual information and facilitating knowledge sharing.  The T-CoT approach offers a new way to refine trajectories within the VLM, potentially improving the quality of trajectory guidance.

*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from the potential to address limitations in current E2E driving systems, such as the lack of common-sense reasoning and interpretability. If SOLVE can effectively leverage VLM knowledge and refine trajectories in a computationally efficient way, it could contribute to more robust and reliable autonomous driving.  The performance gains reported on nuScenes support this potential.  By aligning high-quality VLM outputs with E2E real-time performance, this approach also tackles efficiency issue in previous VLM integrated approaches. The integration of VLM could alleviate causal confusion and improve the interpretability of the autonomous driving results.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Integration:**  The combination of feature-level and trajectory-level integration is a key strength.
    *   **Efficiency Focus:** The temporal decoupling strategy and SQ-Former design are explicitly geared toward computational efficiency, which is crucial for real-world deployment.
    *   **Trajectory Chain of Thought:** This method allows the VLM to provide precise, safe trajectory planning by generating and refining high-quality trajectories, especially in complex and uncertain environments.
    *   **Performance Improvement:** The experimental results on nuScenes demonstrate promising improvements over existing methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Advance:** While the combination of techniques is novel, the individual components (Q-Formers, trajectory refinement, etc.) are based on existing work. The magnitude of improvement is thus partially dependent on careful engineering.
    *   **Dependence on QA Data:** The performance of VLM relies heavily on the quality and diversity of the QA pairs used for training. The paper does not fully address how to construct high-quality QA pairs for the T-CoT process, which is critical for the overall performance.
    *   **Practical Considerations:** The paper does not provide extensive details about computational costs, which are important for assessing the real-world feasibility of SOLVE. How much latency is acceptable for the asynchronous process? Further details on latency and computation cost will further highlight the significance of the proposed work.
    * **Limited generalization study**: The study is done on single dataset. The lack of study on other datasets poses concerns about the generalization ability of the proposed method.

*   **Potential Influence:** If proven to be robust and efficient, SOLVE could influence the direction of research toward more tightly integrated VLM-E2E autonomous driving systems. It also provides valuable insights into feature alignment, trajectory refinement, and temporal decoupling strategies. The T-CoT could be adopted in other reasoning chains.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-motivated and reasonably novel approach to integrating VLMs into E2E autonomous driving. The comprehensive integration strategy, combined with a focus on efficiency, addresses key limitations in existing methods.  The performance improvements reported on nuScenes are encouraging. However, the incremental nature of the individual components, the dependence on high-quality QA training data, the limited practical considerations, and generalization ability pose a concern. The significance is contingent on proving the robustness and efficiency of the integrated system.

### Two-way Evidence self-Alignment based Dual-Gated Reasoning Enhancement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16806v1)
- **Authors**: Kexin Zhang, Junlan Chen, Daifeng Li, Yuxuan Zhang, Yangyang Feng, Bowen Deng, Weixu Chen
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) encounter difficulties in knowledge-intensive multi-step reasoning (KIMSR) tasks. One challenge is how to effectively extract and represent rationale evidence. The current methods often extract semantically relevant but logically irrelevant evidence, resulting in flawed reasoning and inaccurate responses. We propose a two-way evidence self-alignment (TW-ESA) module, which utilizes the mutual alignment between strict reasoning and LLM reasoning to enhance its understanding of the causal logic of evidence, thereby addressing the first challenge. Another challenge is how to utilize the rationale evidence and LLM's intrinsic knowledge for accurate reasoning when the evidence contains uncertainty. We propose a dual-gated reasoning enhancement (DGR) module to gradually fuse useful knowledge of LLM within strict reasoning, which can enable the model to perform accurate reasoning by focusing on causal elements in the evidence and exhibit greater robustness. The two modules are collaboratively trained in a unified framework ESA-DGR. Extensive experiments on three diverse and challenging KIMSR datasets reveal that ESA-DGR significantly surpasses state-of-the-art LLM-based fine-tuning methods, with remarkable average improvements of 4% in exact match (EM) and 5% in F1 score. The implementation code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/ESA-DGR-2BF8.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a two-way evidence self-alignment (TW-ESA) and dual-gated reasoning enhancement (DGR) module called ESA-DGR for knowledge-intensive multi-step reasoning (KIMSR) tasks. TW-ESA addresses the challenge of semantic-logic mismatch in evidence extraction by mutually aligning strict reasoning (RIE) and LLM reasoning. DGR aims to enhance reasoning accuracy and robustness by selectively integrating LLM's intrinsic knowledge with external evidence, especially when the evidence contains uncertainty. The model is trained end-to-end, and experiments on three KIMSR datasets demonstrate improved performance over existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to KIMSR by explicitly addressing the challenges of semantic-logic mismatch and uncertainty-aware hallucination. TW-ESA, incorporating both token-level and hidden-state alignments, seems like a solid contribution to improving the quality of rationale evidence extraction, guiding the extraction by LLMs and further constrain the extraction using strict model. The dual-gated reasoning module is also a reasonable design to balance strict reasoning and LLM's internal knowledge. Using both modules collaboratively to improve KIMSR performs well on experiments and seems to be a good approach for this task.

*   **Significance:** The results demonstrate that ESA-DGR achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple challenging KIMSR datasets, surpassing existing LLM-based fine-tuning methods.  The average improvements in exact match (EM) and F1 score are remarkable. The ablations also show that each module contributes to the performance boost, justifying their inclusion in the architecture.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly identifies two significant limitations in existing KIMSR approaches: semantic-logic mismatch and uncertainty-aware hallucination.
    *   The proposed TW-ESA and DGR modules are well-motivated and technically sound.
    *   The end-to-end training framework and experimental results on diverse datasets provide strong empirical evidence for the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   The ablations and sensitivity analyses offer insights into the contribution of each component.
    *   The visualization of token representations provides a valuable qualitative understanding of how TW-ESA helps to disentangle rationale and non-rationale tokens.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper would benefit from more detailed theoretical analysis of why and how the proposed alignment and gating mechanisms work. While the empirical results are strong, a more rigorous theoretical foundation would strengthen the claims.
    *   The discussion of related work could be more comprehensive. While the authors discuss RAG and evidence extraction techniques, a deeper analysis of other related approaches, particularly those addressing uncertainty in reasoning, would be beneficial.
    *   The conclusion of a rigorous understanding of inner models are still limited at this stage and still require further research, a deeper insight could have been offered in this area.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The work's focus on improving the quality of rationale evidence extraction and reasoning robustness could inspire further research in this area.
    *   The TW-ESA and DGR modules could be adapted and extended to other knowledge-intensive NLP tasks.
    *   The framework could also serve as a benchmark for future research in KIMSR.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper addresses a practical problem of real-world application, especially when considering the complexity and variability of information retrieval and integration in KIMSR. The paper provides strong empirical evidence to justify the approach.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### DeepRec: Towards a Deep Dive Into the Item Space with Large Language Model Based Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16810v1)
- **Authors**: Bowen Zheng, Xiaolei Wang, Enze Liu, Xi Wang, Lu Hongyu, Yu Chen, Wayne Xin Zhao, Ji-Rong Wen
- **Abstract**: Recently, large language models (LLMs) have been introduced into recommender systems (RSs), either to enhance traditional recommendation models (TRMs) or serve as recommendation backbones. However, existing LLM-based RSs often do not fully exploit the complementary advantages of LLMs (e.g., world knowledge and reasoning) and TRMs (e.g., recommendation-specific knowledge and efficiency) to fully explore the item space. To address this, we propose DeepRec, a novel LLM-based RS that enables autonomous multi-turn interactions between LLMs and TRMs for deep exploration of the item space. In each interaction turn, LLMs reason over user preferences and interact with TRMs to retrieve candidate items. After multi-turn interactions, LLMs rank the retrieved items to generate the final recommendations. We adopt reinforcement learning(RL) based optimization and propose novel designs from three aspects: recommendation model based data rollout, recommendation-oriented hierarchical rewards, and a two-stage RL training strategy. For data rollout, we introduce a preference-aware TRM, with which LLMs interact to construct trajectory data. For rewards, we design a hierarchical reward function that involves both process-level and outcome-level rewards to optimize the interaction process and recommendation performance, respectively. For RL training, we develop a two-stage training strategy, where the first stage aims to guide LLMs to interact with TRMs and the second stage focuses on performance improvement. Experiments on public datasets demonstrate that DeepRec significantly outperforms both traditional and LLM-based baselines, offering a new paradigm for deep exploration in recommendation systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DeepRec paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepRec, a novel recommender system that leverages large language models (LLMs) for deep exploration of the item space.  Unlike existing LLM-based approaches that often rely on single-turn interactions or constrain the LLM's generative capabilities, DeepRec proposes a multi-turn, autonomous reasoning-retrieval framework. This framework involves iterative interactions between an LLM and a traditional recommendation model (TRM). The LLM reasons about user preferences and interacts with the TRM to retrieve candidate items in each turn. Reinforcement learning (RL) is used to optimize the interaction process, with carefully designed reward functions and a two-stage training strategy. The results on public datasets demonstrate that DeepRec outperforms both traditional and other LLM-based recommendation baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the multi-turn, autonomous interaction between LLMs and TRMs. Existing LLM-based RSs often treat LLMs as either auxiliary feature enrichers, rerankers, or direct recommenders, limiting their ability to fully exploit both the knowledge of LLMs and the recommendation-specific insights of TRMs. The idea of using TRMs as "tools" for LLMs, inspired by recent advances in LLM-based agents, is relatively new in the recommendation domain. The RL-based optimization, hierarchical reward design, and two-stage training strategy are also novel contributions tailored to this specific interaction paradigm.  The preference-aware TRM that uses the text output of LLMs is also a worthwhile extension.

**Significance:**  The paper's significance stems from addressing a key limitation of current LLM-based RSs: their inability to effectively explore the item space and leverage the complementary strengths of LLMs and TRMs. DeepRec provides a promising new paradigm that can potentially lead to more diverse and relevant recommendations. The framework is general and can be potentially extended to other recommendation scenarios, like knowledge-aware recommendation or conversational recommendation. By demonstrating significant improvements over state-of-the-art baselines, the paper provides a strong empirical justification for its proposed approach.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Architecture:** The autonomous multi-turn interaction framework is a genuinely innovative approach to LLM-based recommendation.
*   **Well-Designed RL Optimization:** The RL framework is thoughtfully designed with carefully chosen reward functions and a two-stage training strategy, which significantly enhances the performance and training stability.
*   **Strong Empirical Evaluation:** The paper presents comprehensive experimental results on multiple datasets, comparing DeepRec with a wide range of competitive baselines. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the proposed approach.
*   **Clear and well explained**. The paper is clear and easy to understand. The contributions are well summarized.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity:** The framework involves multiple components (LLM, TRM, RL optimization), making it relatively complex to implement and train. The paper's ablation experiments mitigate this concern to some extent, but simplifying the architecture would be beneficial.
*   **Computational Cost:** While the paper demonstrates performance improvements, the computational cost of multi-turn interactions and RL training could be a concern for real-world applications. Future work should focus on improving the efficiency of DeepRec.
*   **Overfitting**: The high sparsity of recommender data may make RL training unstable, it will be better if the paper can provide some theoretical insights on that or analyze the sensitivity of different RL hyperparameter, for example, KL coefficients or temperature.
*   **Limited analysis on item retrieval**: One of the core motivations of the work is to deeply explore the item space. However, the analysis of item retrieval is very limited. The paper only shows results of recommendation performance but does not provide any analysis or visualization about the diversity and effectiveness of retrieving items in each turn.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence future research in LLM-based recommendation systems. It introduces a new direction for exploring the item space and provides a valuable framework for combining LLMs with TRMs. Other researchers may build upon DeepRec by developing more efficient training methods, exploring different interaction strategies, or integrating additional knowledge sources.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign the paper a score of **7.5**.

*   The novelty is significant, but the reliance on RL and relatively complex architecture reduces the appeal slightly. It is a good research paper, but not a world-changing one.

Overall, DeepRec represents a significant step forward in LLM-based recommendation, with its innovative multi-turn interaction framework and strong empirical results.  It addresses a key limitation of existing approaches and opens up new avenues for research in this area.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### KTAE: A Model-Free Algorithm to Key-Tokens Advantage Estimation in Mathematical Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16826v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Sun, Wen Yang, Pu Jian, Qianlong Du, Fuwei Cui, Shuo Ren, Jiajun Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances have demonstrated that integrating reinforcement learning with rule-based rewards can significantly enhance the reasoning capabilities of large language models, even without supervised fine-tuning. However, prevalent reinforcement learning algorithms such as GRPO and its variants like DAPO, suffer from a coarse granularity issue when computing the advantage. Specifically, they compute rollout-level advantages that assign identical values to every token within a sequence, failing to capture token-specific contributions and hindering effective learning. To address this limitation, we propose Key-token Advantage Estimation (KTAE) - a novel algorithm that estimates fine-grained, token-level advantages without introducing additional models. KTAE leverages the correctness of sampled rollouts and applies statistical analysis to quantify the importance of individual tokens within a sequence to the final outcome. This quantified token-level importance is then combined with the rollout-level advantage to obtain a more fine-grained token-level advantage estimation. Empirical results show that models trained with GRPO+KTAE and DAPO+KTAE outperform baseline methods across five mathematical reasoning benchmarks. Notably, they achieve higher accuracy with shorter responses and even surpass R1-Distill-Qwen-1.5B using the same base model.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Key-token Advantage Estimation (KTAE), a model-free algorithm designed to improve the performance of Reinforcement Learning (RL) techniques, specifically GRPO and DAPO, when applied to mathematical reasoning tasks. KTAE addresses the issue of coarse-grained advantage estimation in existing methods, where every token in a rollout receives the same advantage value.  KTAE uses statistical analysis (Fisher's exact test and Information Gain) of token occurrence in correct vs. incorrect rollouts to estimate token-level advantages. It combines this token-level information with the rollout-level advantage from GRPO/DAPO to create a more refined advantage signal.  The paper demonstrates that integrating KTAE with GRPO and DAPO leads to improved performance on several mathematical reasoning benchmarks, with shorter response lengths, even surpassing a larger R1-Distill-Qwen-1.5B model using the same base model. The algorithm aims to provide finer-grained advantage information without introducing extra models, resulting in lower training costs. It also quantifies the importance differences between tokens using statistical methods, offering strong interpretability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using token-level analysis to refine advantage estimation in RL for language models is moderately novel. While process-level rewards have been explored, KTAE offers a model-free approach, which is a key distinguishing factor.  The use of statistical methods like Fisher's exact test and Information Gain to quantify token importance is a clever way to achieve finer granularity without training additional models. The algorithm’s simplicity, with its plug-and-play nature, is also a key benefit. However, similar techniques in different domains could temper claims of extreme novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in addressing a practical limitation of existing RL algorithms for language models: the coarse granularity of advantage estimation. By improving the training signal at the token level, KTAE helps models focus on relevant information and generate more concise, accurate answers. The empirical results, showcasing improvements across several mathematical reasoning benchmarks, provide solid evidence of its effectiveness. The ability to outperform a larger distilled model is particularly noteworthy. The reduced response length is important because it improves the quality of the generated response. The model focuses on key tokens which results in it solving the problem more efficiently. The potential to generalize to other tasks beyond mathematical reasoning is mentioned but requires further validation, which limits the significance somewhat.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Model-free approach:** Avoids the complexity and cost of training additional reward models.
    *   **Clear methodology:** Well-defined algorithm with a solid rationale.
    *   **Strong empirical results:** Demonstrates consistent improvements across multiple benchmarks.
    *   **Improved efficiency:** Reduces response length without sacrificing accuracy.
    *   **Interpretability:** Token-level advantages allow for understanding which tokens are most important.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** Primarily focused on mathematical reasoning tasks with binary rewards; generalization to other domains and reward structures needs further exploration.
    *   **Dependency on Rule-Based Rewards:** KTAE relies on the correctness signal generated by rule-based rewards, which might be brittle or difficult to design for more complex tasks. Although the algorithm does improve model performance for incorrect rewards, it depends on a rule-based reward to even estimate this.
    *   **Modest Novelty:** While effective, the approach builds upon existing RL algorithms and uses well-established statistical methods.
    *   **Limited Hyperparameter sensitivity:** This is a strength and weakness. While its simplicity is a positive, it doesn't explore the benefits of tuning the variables appropriately and there is no theoretical analysis of how its variables impact the model performance.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in RL for language models by highlighting the importance of fine-grained advantage estimation. The model-free nature of KTAE could encourage further exploration of similar techniques in other domains and reward settings. Future work might involve adapting KTAE to other tasks, such as code generation, and exploring its robustness to different types of reward functions.

Score: 7.5

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by addressing a practical limitation in RL for language models and proposing a novel, model-free solution. The empirical results demonstrate the effectiveness of KTAE in improving performance and efficiency on mathematical reasoning tasks. However, the limited scope of the experiments, dependence on rule-based rewards, and the modest novelty compared to some foundational works in the field temper the overall score. Nevertheless, KTAE provides a solid foundation for future research in fine-grained advantage estimation and has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and accurate RL-trained language models.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unlearning Isn't Deletion: Investigating Reversibility of Machine Unlearning in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16831v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyu Xu, Xiang Yue, Yang Liu, Qingqing Ye, Haibo Hu, Minxin Du
- **Abstract**: Unlearning in large language models (LLMs) is intended to remove the influence of specific data, yet current evaluations rely heavily on token-level metrics such as accuracy and perplexity. We show that these metrics can be misleading: models often appear to forget, but their original behavior can be rapidly restored with minimal fine-tuning, revealing that unlearning may obscure information rather than erase it. To diagnose this phenomenon, we introduce a representation-level evaluation framework using PCA-based similarity and shift, centered kernel alignment, and Fisher information. Applying this toolkit across six unlearning methods, three domains (text, code, math), and two open-source LLMs, we uncover a critical distinction between reversible and irreversible forgetting. In reversible cases, models suffer token-level collapse yet retain latent features; in irreversible cases, deeper representational damage occurs. We further provide a theoretical account linking shallow weight perturbations near output layers to misleading unlearning signals, and show that reversibility is modulated by task type and hyperparameters. Our findings reveal a fundamental gap in current evaluation practices and establish a new diagnostic foundation for trustworthy unlearning in LLMs. We provide a unified toolkit for analyzing LLM representation changes under unlearning and relearning: https://github.com/XiaoyuXU1/Representational_Analysis_Tools.git.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Unlearning Isn't Deletion: Investigating Reversibility of Machine Unlearning in LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper challenges the current evaluation paradigm for machine unlearning in Large Language Models (LLMs), which primarily relies on token-level metrics. The authors argue that these metrics can be misleading because models often appear to forget data, yet the original behavior can be rapidly restored with minimal fine-tuning, suggesting that unlearning only obscures information rather than erasing it.  They introduce a representation-level evaluation framework, encompassing PCA-based similarity and shift, centered kernel alignment (CKA), and Fisher information, to diagnose this phenomenon. Using this toolkit across multiple unlearning methods, datasets, and LLMs, the authors distinguish between reversible and irreversible forgetting. Reversible forgetting involves token-level collapse with retained latent features, while irreversible forgetting entails deeper representational damage. They provide a theoretical explanation linking shallow weight perturbations near the output layer to misleading unlearning signals, and demonstrate that reversibility is influenced by task type and hyperparameters. The authors introduce a unified toolkit for analyzing LLM representation changes under unlearning and relearning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the recognition that current unlearning evaluations are superficial and fail to capture the true state of learned information within LLMs. Moving beyond token-level metrics is a significant and necessary step. The introduction and application of representation-level diagnostics (PCA similarity/shift, CKA, and Fisher information) provide a new, more nuanced way to assess unlearning effectiveness. The concept of reversible vs. irreversible forgetting is a valuable conceptual contribution.

*   **Significance:**  The paper has significant implications for the trustworthiness of machine unlearning in LLMs.  If unlearning only superficially obscures information, it raises serious concerns about privacy and compliance, especially in sensitive applications. By identifying reversible and irreversible types of forgetting and linking these to model characteristics and unlearning methods, the paper provides a foundation for developing more robust unlearning strategies. The provision of a unified toolkit is highly valuable for other researchers to reproduce and extend this analysis.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper effectively highlights the limitations of current unlearning evaluations and the dangers of relying solely on token-level metrics.
    *   **Comprehensive Methodology:** The introduced representation-level evaluation framework is well-defined, practical, and relatively comprehensive. It brings established representation similarity metrics from other areas to this important problem.
    *   **Extensive Empirical Validation:**  The empirical evaluation, covering a range of unlearning methods, datasets, and LLMs, strengthens the paper's claims and demonstrates the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The theoretical analysis provides a plausible explanation for the observed reversibility phenomenon, linking it to the nature of weight perturbations.
    *   **Practical Contribution:** The provided toolkit enables researchers to easily reproduce the key claims and helps to push the state-of-the-art in unlearning research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Theoretical Analysis:** While the theoretical analysis provides some insight, it's relatively high-level and could benefit from more rigorous mathematical treatment, especially regarding the conditions under which perturbations remain shallow vs. become distributed across layers.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The representation-level diagnostics can be computationally expensive, particularly for very large LLMs, potentially limiting the scalability of this approach.
    *   **No Practical Unlearning Improvements:** The paper focuses primarily on *diagnosing* the problem of reversibility, not on *solving* it. While the insights offer guidance for developing better unlearning methods, the paper doesn't present a new unlearning algorithm that demonstrably avoids reversible forgetting.
    *   **Some hyper-parameter tuning results have high variance in test scores due to continual unlearning with fixed learning rate.**
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a substantial influence on the field of machine unlearning in LLMs. It will encourage researchers to adopt more rigorous evaluation practices, focusing on the representational impact of unlearning rather than simply token-level performance. It could spur the development of new unlearning methods designed to achieve genuine erasure rather than superficial obfuscation.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a valuable and insightful contribution by identifying a critical flaw in current unlearning evaluations and proposing a practical solution. The use of representation similarity diagnostics and the conceptual framework it gives rise to, offers tangible improvements to the field. However, the diagnostic nature of the work, while valuable, limits its transformative potential. Furthermore, a more rigorous mathematical treatment of the causes of reversible/irreversible forgetting would have further strengthened the paper's contribution. Considering the originality of the central insight, the solid methodology, thorough empirical work and the limitation in improving the state-of-the-art unlearning methods, a score of 7 is warranted.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### From EduVisBench to EduVisAgent: A Benchmark and Multi-Agent Framework for Pedagogical Visualization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16832v1)
- **Authors**: Haonian Ji, Shi Qiu, Siyang Xin, Siwei Han, Zhaorun Chen, Hongyi Wang, Dake Zhang, Huaxiu Yao
- **Abstract**: While foundation models (FMs), such as diffusion models and large vision-language models (LVLMs), have been widely applied in educational contexts, their ability to generate pedagogically effective visual explanations remains limited. Most existing approaches focus primarily on textual reasoning, overlooking the critical role of structured and interpretable visualizations in supporting conceptual understanding. To better assess the visual reasoning capabilities of FMs in educational settings, we introduce EduVisBench, a multi-domain, multi-level benchmark. EduVisBench features diverse STEM problem sets requiring visually grounded solutions, along with a fine-grained evaluation rubric informed by pedagogical theory. Our empirical analysis reveals that existing models frequently struggle with the inherent challenge of decomposing complex reasoning and translating it into visual representations aligned with human cognitive processes. To address these limitations, we propose EduVisAgent, a multi-agent collaborative framework that coordinates specialized agents for instructional planning, reasoning decomposition, metacognitive prompting, and visualization design. Experimental results show that EduVisAgent substantially outperforms all baselines, achieving a 40.2% improvement and delivering more educationally aligned visualizations. EduVisBench and EduVisAgent are available at https://github.com/aiming-lab/EduVisBench and https://github.com/aiming-lab/EduVisAgent.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EduVisBench, a new benchmark for evaluating the ability of foundation models (FMs) to generate pedagogically effective visual explanations for STEM problems.  It also proposes EduVisAgent, a multi-agent framework designed to overcome limitations identified in existing FMs, particularly their difficulty in decomposing complex reasoning into visual representations that align with human cognitive processes. EduVisAgent uses a collaborative approach with specialized agents for instructional planning, reasoning decomposition, metacognitive prompting, and visualization design. Experiments demonstrate that EduVisAgent outperforms existing baselines in generating educationally aligned visualizations. The benchmark and framework are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Novel Benchmark:**  The creation of EduVisBench fills a gap in the evaluation of FMs by focusing specifically on visual pedagogical reasoning.  The fine-grained evaluation rubric is a significant contribution, moving beyond simple accuracy to assess interpretability, cognitive alignment, and instructional clarity.
    * **Multi-Agent Framework:** EduVisAgent offers a structured approach to generating visual explanations. The division of labor among specialized agents mimics the collaborative reasoning process of expert instructional designers. The modularity of the framework also makes it easier to analyze the contribution of each agent.
    * **Empirical Results:**  The experimental results demonstrate a substantial performance improvement with EduVisAgent compared to existing FMs and a specialized visualization agent. This provides strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    * **Public Availability:** Releasing both the benchmark and framework fosters reproducibility and allows other researchers to build upon this work.
    * **Addresses an Important Problem:** Visual learning aids are crucial for STEM education, particularly in K-12 settings. Evaluating and improving FM capabilities in this area is a relevant and timely research question.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on GPT-40 for Evaluation:**  While the authors standardize and image the outputs, and use a detailed rubric to guide scoring, relying on GPT-40 as an evaluator introduces a potential bias and raises questions about its understanding of pedagogical principles. A fully human evaluation, even on a subset of the data, would strengthen the results.
    * **Limited Scope of Baselines:** While a range of FMs are evaluated, the baseline models are those publicly available and quickly become outdated. Also, the baselines and proposed methods are evaluated on the generated image outputs instead of comparing all the intermediate steps or the computational costs.
    * **Generalizability:** The benchmark focuses on a specific set of STEM domains (Maths, Physics, Chemistry).  While these are important, the generalizability of EduVisAgent to other educational areas (e.g., humanities, social sciences) is unclear.
    * **Complexity:** The framework seems fairly complex to implement, which may limit its widespread adoption.

* **Novelty:**  The paper is novel in its specific focus on visual pedagogical reasoning for FMs and the introduction of a multi-agent architecture to address this problem. While individual components (e.g., reasoning decomposition, visualization agents) have been explored in other contexts, their combination within the EduVisAgent framework is novel.

* **Significance:** The paper is significant because it highlights the limitations of current FMs in a critical educational domain. EduVisBench provides a valuable tool for assessing and improving the visual learning capabilities of AI systems.  EduVisAgent offers a promising approach to generating more effective visual explanations, with the potential to improve student understanding and learning outcomes.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of **7**.

* **7 represents a solid contribution with clear novelty and significance but with certain limitations.**
* EduVisBench is a valuable benchmark that addresses a critical need. EduVisAgent is a well-designed framework with strong experimental results.
* The primary weaknesses are the reliance on GPT-40 for evaluation, the limited scope of baselines, and potential generalizability concerns.  These issues prevent a higher score.
* While not revolutionary, the paper offers a substantial advance in the application of AI to education and has the potential to influence future research in this area.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### SimpleDeepSearcher: Deep Information Seeking via Web-Powered Reasoning Trajectory Synthesis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16834v1)
- **Authors**: Shuang Sun, Huatong Song, Yuhao Wang, Ruiyang Ren, Jinhao Jiang, Junjie Zhang, Fei Bai, Jia Deng, Wayne Xin Zhao, Zheng Liu, Lei Fang, Zhongyuan Wang, Ji-Rong Wen
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems have advanced large language models (LLMs) in complex deep search scenarios requiring multi-step reasoning and iterative information retrieval. However, existing approaches face critical limitations that lack high-quality training trajectories or suffer from the distributional mismatches in simulated environments and prohibitive computational costs for real-world deployment. This paper introduces SimpleDeepSearcher, a lightweight yet effective framework that bridges this gap through strategic data engineering rather than complex training paradigms. Our approach synthesizes high-quality training data by simulating realistic user interactions in live web search environments, coupled with a multi-criteria curation strategy that optimizes the diversity and quality of input and output side. Experiments on five benchmarks across diverse domains demonstrate that SFT on only 871 curated samples yields significant improvements over RL-based baselines. Our work establishes SFT as a viable pathway by systematically addressing the data-scarce bottleneck, offering practical insights for efficient deep search systems.
